Yeah, and I second language.# Incident and Local Architecture Regression Catalog

Last reviewed: 2026-08-15

This file records the failures observed while bringing the multi-client SQL sync system to its current design. It is a regression catalog, not a live operations log. Real client databases must remain out of automated verification; all architecture tests use fake local SQL Server clients.

## Current architecture and safety contract

Each Windows client captures only committed SQL Server Change Tracking operations after its durable cursor. Clients upload bounded chunks to the control plane. The server waits at the multi-writer upload barrier, combines operations from every participating client, chooses a durable latest-change winner, and exposes the merged result to every client. Each target applies the result through an atomic staging transaction and advances its cursor only after the complete participating batch succeeds.

The non-negotiable invariants are:

- A full snapshot, row-count difference, offline client, failed upload, comparison, or reconciliation may never imply deletion.
- Only an explicit Change Tracking `D` operation with a complete permanent primary key may delete a row.
- A unique/business-key collision may atomically replace only the older conflicting identity when deterministic latest-change metadata and the unique-key definition are available.
- An interrupted operation is all-or-nothing and safe to retry.
- An offline client does not block online peers; shared cursors wait until the offline participant catches up.
- Automatic scheduling, manual Sync All, recovery, and client updates are separate controls.
- At most one client per owner can be the conflict source. It wins only conflicting row/business-key identities; with no selection, the durable latest committed change wins.
- Every upload reads a fixed Change Tracking version-bounded database snapshot before network transmission; later database writes wait for the next sync job.

## Local verification

Run the normal architecture gate from the repository root:

```powershell
.\tests\run_sync_verification.ps1 -Profile Standard
```

It runs Flutter analysis/unit tests, repository contracts, updater path checks, a three-client SQL Server 2022 suite, atomic fault injection, concurrency, fuzzing, 5,000-row scale, SQL restart recovery, and a bounded soak.

Use the full SQL-version matrix when SQL syntax or version-sensitive behavior changes:

```powershell
.\tests\run_sync_verification.ps1 -Profile All
```

Fast focused checks are also available:

```powershell
flutter test
python tests/test_incident_regressions.py
.\tests\docker-sync\run.ps1 -Suite standard
```

The Coverage column is enforced by `tests/test_incident_regressions.py`. A referenced selector must remain in the referenced test source.

| INC-001 | Velvet Home saw a database while Velvet Factory did not; access grants failed on some installations. | SQL instance discovery, Windows login authorization, instance mismatch, legacy SQL syntax, and broken attachments were previously treated as one failure. | Discover every non-system database, explain its exact access state, generate a standard UAC grant flow, and continue after an individual instance failure. | Database discovery and access diagnostics were separated and made installation-agnostic. | `sync_windows_agent/test/database_access_test.dart`<br>`tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_windows_client_database_access_failure_is_actionable_not_periodic` |
| INC-002 | The client closed immediately on some Windows installations. | An optional DWM theme API was called as though it always existed, producing native exception `0xc0000005`. | Optional Windows APIs must be dynamically loaded, null-checked, and released before startup continues. | The native runner now guards `DwmSetWindowAttribute`. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_optional_windows_theme_api_is_guarded` |
| INC-003 | Empty command prompts flashed repeatedly. | Repo-owned historical launch paths used console processes; the latest observed flashes were an external FlutterFalcon builder using `Win32_Process.Create` with `cmd.exe /c` and no hidden startup information. | Repo-owned supervisor/updater processes must start hidden and must not use Dart `cmd.exe` launchers. External FlutterFalcon must be fixed in its own launcher and must not persist access credentials in temporary scripts. | SQL Sync launchers are hidden; the external ownership boundary and remediation are documented. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_repo_owned_background_launchers_are_hidden`<br>`tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_windows_agent_can_apply_server_requested_client_updates` |
| INC-004 | Client update checks appeared every few seconds and could reopen processes. | Update responsibility was split between the app, supervisor, and updater without a stable ownership rule. | The healthy app performs lightweight checks; the supervisor performs a slow fallback check and starts hidden; only strictly newer versions install. | Update ownership, retry, and monotonic-version rules were consolidated. | `tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_shell_auto_update_stays_active_for_logged_in_clients`<br>`sync_windows_agent/test/client_version_test.dart` |
| INC-005 | Updates failed for paths containing spaces/apostrophes or packaged stale local AOT/localhost URLs. | Raw `-File` arguments and stale `.dart_tool/flutter_build` output crossed release boundaries. | Encode unsafe paths, clear stale AOT output, embed the live control-plane URL, and verify the portable startup manifest. | Packaging and updater contracts now fail closed. | `tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_update_script_encodes_paths_passed_to_hidden_powershell`<br>`tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_windows_client_build_clears_stale_flutter_aot_cache`<br>`tests/test_windows_updater_paths.ps1` |
| INC-006 | Sync history showed several operations every minute and Sync All could overlap existing work. | Scheduler ticks, per-client upload/download jobs, and manual queues were conflated; active work was not always reused as a barrier. | One logical batch may contain multiple jobs, but duplicate manual batches must defer and cancellation must clear the durable queue. | Batch barriers, bounded preflight, and queue cancellation were added. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_manual_sync_all_defers_when_owner_has_active_batch_work`<br>`tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_cancel_active_sync_also_clears_durable_manual_queue` |
| INC-007 | “Catching up” was confusing while automatic sync was paused. | Automatic scheduling state and already accepted/manual recovery work were displayed as one state. | Pausing the scheduler must not corrupt accepted work; offline debt must say whether catch-up is paused or active. | Scheduler pause and catch-up status are now explicit. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_paused_automatic_sync_does_not_stop_an_accepted_manual_queue`<br>`tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_offline_debt_status_distinguishes_paused_from_active_catchup` |
| INC-008 | Tables from the wrong database could enter planning. | Policies and jobs were not consistently scoped to the agent's selected database. | Every planned batch is scoped to the selected database with a safe database-agnostic fallback only for legacy policy data. | Selected-database scoping was added end to end. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_sync_scheduling_is_scoped_to_each_agents_selected_database`<br>`sync_windows_agent/test/database_selection_test.dart` |
| INC-009 | It was unclear whether every client could be a source. | Earlier source-oriented language obscured the multi-writer upload barrier. | Every participating client uploads its changes; the server combines them; every client downloads the merged result. | Protocol-v4 multi-writer union is the normal path. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_multi_writer_batch_has_upload_barrier_and_merged_download`<br>`tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_multi_writer_heartbeat_exposes_upload_and_download_for_same_table` |
| INC-010 | An offline client risked blocking other clients or losing its cursor. | Partial-client batches could advance the shared cursor too early. | Online peers continue normally; upload/download cursors stay unchanged until the offline participant catches up. | Partial-batch cursor debt is durable and retryable. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_partial_batches_preserve_cursors_until_offline_clients_catch_up`<br>`sync_windows_agent/test/change_tracking_cursor_policy_test.dart`<br>`tests/docker-sync/run_scenarios.py::offline-peer-online-continuity-and-reconnect-catch-up` |
| INC-011 | Rows disappeared from client databases during snapshot/reconciliation work. | Snapshot absence was previously interpreted as replacement/deletion. | Full snapshots, bootstrap, comparison, and reconciliation are insert/update-only and preserve target-only rows. | Table-wide replacement paths were removed. | `sync_windows_agent/test/sql_sync_merge_test.dart`<br>`tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_windows_client_target_apply_uses_atomic_staging_without_missing_row_deletes`<br>`tests/docker-sync/run_scenarios.py::complete-reconcile-preserves-target-only-unicode-retry` |
| INC-012 | It was unclear whether an Al-Ameen/user deletion should synchronize. | User intent was incorrectly mixed with transport safety. | A committed SQL deletion from either source is equivalent and synchronizes only as an explicit durable tombstone for the exact primary key. | Tombstone-only deletion is enforced. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_only_explicit_delta_tombstones_can_delete`<br>`sync_windows_agent/test/sql_sync_merge_test.dart`<br>`tests/docker-sync/run_scenarios.py::explicit-delete-of-missing-key-is-idempotent` |
| INC-013 | Power loss, connection loss, SQL restart, or a lost response could leave partial data. | Apply and cursor advancement were not uniformly treated as one retry-safe state machine. | Apply is atomic; faults roll back; a committed response loss is idempotent; cursors advance only after success. | Atomic staging and idempotent operation IDs are mandatory. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_change_tracking_transport_and_table_apply_are_atomic`<br>`tests/docker-sync/run_scenarios.py::fault-before-commit-rollback`<br>`tests/docker-sync/run_scenarios.py::commit-response-loss-idempotent-retry` |
| INC-014 | Large tables were repeatedly uploaded in full and took too long. | Bootstrap/recovery snapshots and ordinary Change Tracking deltas were not clearly separated; transport chunks were too broad. | Normal sync uploads only changes; full anti-entropy is explicit; all uploads are bounded and resumable. | Delta checkpoints, chunking, and retained recovery were added. | `tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_protocol_v5_multi_writer_is_hashed_delta_only_and_fails_closed_without_baseline`<br>`tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_snapshot_upload_job_advances_through_start_progress_and_chunk_upload_only`<br>`tests/docker-sync/run_scenarios.py::large-1200-row-batch` |
| INC-015 | A unique-key conflict asked for user input even when latest-wins was configured. | Retryable SQL unique conflicts were classified as permanent decisions. | With deterministic metadata, latest-wins retries automatically and the UI must not claim a decision is needed. | Automatic conflict repair and decision counts were separated. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_unique_key_conflicts_become_automatic_latest_change_retries`<br>`tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_unique_business_key_failure_is_retryable_without_user_input` |
| INC-016 | Full union still failed when different primary keys shared the same SQL unique key. | Unique-key coalescing was applied to deltas but not canonical full snapshots, and server unique metadata was dropped. | Canonical union must preserve unique definitions and resolve the collision without a partial apply. | Full-snapshot metadata and atomic rollback were added; INC-213 later superseded identity replacement with fail-closed, audited number reservation so different documents are both preserved. | `sync_windows_agent/test/sql_sync_merge_test.dart`<br>`tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_latest_change_winner_is_durable_across_batches_and_resettable`<br>`tests/docker-sync/run_scenarios.py::business-key-collision-fails-closed-then-reserved-key-applies` |
| INC-017 | “Authoritative source” sounded like replacing an entire database. | A one-way repair control was exposed beside normal multi-writer sync and could be mistaken for normal conflict policy. | Normal sync remains multi-writer latest-wins; no client list action may offer destructive whole-database replacement. | The destructive client-list option was removed and retained recovery is non-destructive. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_clients_list_does_not_offer_destructive_authoritative_source`<br>`tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_windows_agent_full_reconciliation_is_non_destructive_and_latest_conflicts_are_atomic` |
| INC-018 | Comparison failed or could not show both clients' rows. | Comparison assumed two clients and mixed winner mutation with read-only diagnostics. | Comparison is read-only, primary-key scoped, paged, and supports multiple clients without advancing sync cursors. | A multi-client comparison request and dialog were added. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_table_row_comparison_is_scoped_read_only_and_multi_client`<br>`tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_windows_comparison_snapshot_is_full_and_preserves_tracking_cursor` |
| INC-019 | The web UI said a table needed a decision while automatic repair was running. | Repairing and decision-required states contributed to the same count/message. | Only genuine user decisions block; automatic repair reports progress and clears itself after verification. | Decision and resolving counts/messages were separated. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_automatic_repair_finishes_for_online_participants_without_user_input`<br>`tests/test_control_plane_perf_contracts.py::test_only_user_decisions_skip_repair_finalization_scan` |
| INC-020 | Cancelling or resetting sync left queued jobs/storage that could restart later. | Active jobs, waiting relay jobs, durable manual queues, epochs, and retained rows were cleaned independently. | Cancel/reset atomically stops all active states, clears the durable queue, and rotates protocol state before new work. | Cancellation and reset are server-enforced. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_server_reset_cancellation_is_enforced_by_server_and_windows_client`<br>`tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_cancel_active_sync_also_clears_durable_manual_queue` |
| INC-021 | Initial Change Tracking version zero was treated as invalid and caused unnecessary full reconciliation. | Baseline validity used `<= 0` rather than distinguishing unavailable/expired from a valid initial version. | Version zero is valid; only unavailable or expired baselines fail closed into explicit recovery. | Baseline parsing and diagnostics were corrected. | `tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_change_tracking_baselines_accept_enabled_initial_version_zero`<br>`sync_windows_agent/test/automatic_change_discovery_test.dart` |
| INC-022 | Arabic/Unicode, control characters, floating-point, binary, identity, computed, and rowversion values could be corrupted or written incorrectly. | Console code pages and generic string serialization were lossy; non-writable SQL columns were not consistently excluded. | Transport is code-page independent and type-aware; computed/rowversion columns are never written. | Framed UTF-16 hex and type-specific SQL formatting were added. | `sync_windows_agent/test/sql_cmd_output_test.dart`<br>`sync_windows_agent/test/sql_sync_schema_test.dart`<br>`tests/docker-sync/run_scenarios.py::exact-unicode-arabic-emoji-cjk` |

| INC-027 | Previously fixed issues could be forgotten or regress without a durable documentation-and-test requirement. | Incident documentation and regression coverage existed, but the repository rules did not require every future issue to update both. | Every issue must be recorded with its symptom, root cause, required behavior, resolution, and automated coverage; its regression test must run in the appropriate standard test gate. | `AGENTS.md` now makes incident documentation and automated regression coverage a completion requirement. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_agents_requires_issue_documentation_and_automated_regression` |
| INC-028 | Local tests were split between several commands, and long Docker runs could open visible Windows processes. | The existing verification gate was complete but had no single documented workstation-safe entry point. | One standard command must run the complete local gate in a hidden child process, retain logs/results, and clearly document profiles, coverage, invariants, and safety boundaries. | Added `tests/run_local_test_standard.ps1` and the local test standard guide. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_local_test_standard_is_hidden_documented_and_delegates_to_gate`<br>`docs/local-testing.md` |
| INC-029 | A repeated local Docker run could fail during initialization because an interrupted prior run left stale SQL tables in the disposable volume. | The harness started its fixed Compose service without first removing orphaned harness state. | Each non-external run must reset only its own disposable SQL volume before starting and must never touch live or external databases. | The Docker harness now runs Compose `down -v` before `up -d`; the cleanup is covered by a contract test. | `tests/test_docker_sync_harness.py::test_local_sql_harness_resets_only_its_disposable_volume_before_start`<br>`tests/docker-sync/run_scenarios.py` |
| INC-030 | The web dashboard showed `TRU database operation budget exceeded` while listing clients with many tables. | `live_state` queried active jobs separately for every displayed client table, exhausting the 256-operation request budget. | Dashboard reads must stay bounded regardless of the number of displayed tables and return the same client/table runtime status. | `live_state` now loads active jobs once and resolves all client and table states in memory. | `tests/test_control_plane_perf_contracts.py::test_live_state_preloads_active_jobs_instead_of_querying_per_table` |
| INC-031 | On mobile, the client filter and sort controls consumed a separate full-width row above the client table. | The desktop filter toolbar was stacked responsively instead of using a mobile-specific compact control. | Mobile clients must expose filtering and sorting from a compact control in the `All clients` card header; desktop keeps its inline toolbar. | Added a 34-pixel header filter button with an active indicator and a keyboard-safe filter/sort bottom sheet. | `tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_mobile_client_filter_is_compact_and_integrated_in_card_header` |
| INC-032 | Hundreds of stale SQL Sync Agent icons accumulated in the Windows notification tray while only one client process was running. | A stale portable executable was published under a newer manifest version, and the client exited immediately after launching a detached updater; the supervisor restarted it before update ownership was established, repeating every retry interval. | Only the updater may stop/restart the client after it has verified and staged a real update, and publishing must fail when the executable version differs from the pubspec/manifest version. | Removed client-side post-launch exit and added an exact packaged executable version gate to the Windows client publisher. | `tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_windows_updater_owns_shutdown_and_published_version_matches_binary` |
| INC-033 | Choosing **No — close the app** on `alshallan2` closed the window briefly, but the client reopened automatically. | The independent supervisor treated a clean user-requested exit exactly like a crash and restarted the executable on its next 15-second check. | A deliberate close must remain stopped across supervisor checks and updates; manually launching the client must clear that state and restore crash supervision. | The native close path now writes a durable user-stopped marker, the supervisor honors it, and a manual non-supervisor launch clears it. | `tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_user_close_pauses_supervisor_until_manual_client_launch` |
| INC-034 | Operators could not keep a registered client out of synchronization from the clients list, and an orphaned `alshallan2--sql8` agent remained visible after its account was gone. | The server already stored `syncEnabled`, but the clients table did not expose it; disabling also did not terminate already queued batches, and account-only deletion could not remove an orphan Agent row. | Every client has a permission-checked Active checkbox; inactive clients are excluded from all new sync work and any active batch involving them is cancelled atomically. Only offline orphan registrations may be removed directly, without deleting SQL data or sync history. | Added the clients-list Active control, batch-safe disable behavior, and a scoped orphan-registration cleanup endpoint. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_disabled_clients_are_excluded_from_protocol_v2_barriers`<br>`tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_orphan_client_registration_deletion_is_scoped_and_refuses_live_accounts`<br>`tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_web_workspace_has_dashboard_and_client_log_navigation` |
| INC-035 | A table that had a disabled server policy remained disabled even after the Windows client proved that its primary key, column types, access, and Change Tracking were safe. | Automatic enrollment created policies only for first-time tables and returned an existing disabled policy unchanged; only a later local row change could reactivate it. | Every eligibility pass must converge an eligible table to enabled immediately; unsafe tables remain blocked with their precise diagnostic and no sync job is guessed or forced. | The idempotent auto-enrollment endpoint now reactivates existing disabled policies as well as creating missing policies. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_eligible_table_auto_enrollment_reactivates_disabled_policies`<br>`tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_first_time_eligible_tables_are_enrolled_automatically` |
| INC-036 | A monitored union bootstrap failed for `AmnDb048::Connections` on both live clients with one incoming row reported as unapplied, although the atomic apply had deliberately protected a newer local edit. | Complete-snapshot verification compared every incoming row after apply but did not exempt the exact number of post-upload local rows that the serializable apply reported as protected. | A hot row changed after upload must remain untouched, keep its Change Tracking delta queued, and must not fail an otherwise atomic bootstrap; any additional unexplained mismatch still fails closed. | Complete-snapshot verification now permits only the reported protected-upsert count and explicitly defers those rows to the next delta. | `tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_complete_snapshot_verification_accepts_only_protected_hot_rows`<br>`sync_windows_agent/test/sql_sync_merge_test.dart::complete snapshot verification defers only protected hot rows` |
| INC-037 | After the user deliberately closed `alshallan2`, the app could reopen later even though the 15-second crash supervisor respected the user-stopped marker. | The supervisor's independent update check and the updater's final restart did not honor the durable user-stopped marker, and a duplicate launch could clear the marker before the single-instance check rejected it. | A deliberate close must suppress crash restarts, background update launches, update-finalizer restarts, startup-shortcut relaunches, and duplicate-launch races. Only a successful manual single-instance launch may clear the marker; an update may install while stopped but must remain stopped afterward. | The supervisor now skips independent updates while user-stopped, both updater paths preserve the stopped state, and the native runner acquires the instance lock before a manual launch can clear the marker. | `tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_user_close_pauses_supervisor_until_manual_client_launch`<br>`tests/test_windows_supervisor.ps1` |
| INC-038 | Downloading the current Windows client from the web opened an older client version even though a newer release had already been published. | The web button and release manifest used the mutable `sync_windows_agent_latest.zip` alias without cache-busting, and the frontend did not send no-cache headers for that alias. A browser or intermediary could therefore reuse an old ZIP. | Human downloads must bypass caches, mutable update aliases and manifests must be served with no-cache headers, and each release manifest must point to its immutable versioned ZIP. | The web download now adds a release nonce, mutable client artifacts receive no-store headers, and the publisher writes the exact versioned ZIP URL into `latest.json`. | `tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_web_header_cache_busts_the_mutable_windows_client_download` |
| INC-039 | A 14,030-row baseline took more than 20 minutes and remained in `applying` long after the peer completed. | Snapshot staging launched a new `sqlcmd` process and SQL connection for every 100 rows, causing roughly 140 sequential reconnects before the set-based atomic merge. | Large snapshot apply must retain SQL Server's 1,000-row `VALUES` bound while loading all bounded statements through one input script and one SQL connection; normal post-baseline sync must still use Change Tracking deltas. | Staging now builds one input-file script containing bounded 1,000-row insert statements, launches `sqlcmd` once, and records stage row, statement, launch, and elapsed diagnostics before the unchanged atomic merge. | `sync_windows_agent/test/sql_sync_merge_test.dart::large snapshot staging uses one script with bounded SQL statements`<br>`tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_windows_snapshot_apply_uses_only_permanent_primary_identity` |
| INC-040 | Production image preparation filled the workstation drive before Docker started. | A manual recursive copy of `backend/server` followed the multi-gigabyte Rust `target/` cache instead of honoring the Docker ignore boundary. | Production contexts must contain only tracked backend, root business, and frontend sources, preserve the current client release artifacts, use immutable exact-commit tags, and never copy local build/cache directories. | Added one exclude-by-construction image builder that creates contexts with `git archive`, overlays only the current client artifacts, builds and pushes both immutable images locally, and cleans its verified workspace-only temporary context. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_production_image_builder_excludes_local_caches_by_construction` |
| INC-041 | `alshallan2` stayed online and heartbeating every 15 seconds, but targeted update and diagnostics requests remained pending for more than 15 minutes. | The heartbeat response both reused an earlier Agent snapshot and re-applied the offline-age gate even though the authenticated heartbeat itself proves the client is online; either timing boundary could hide a command from the polling client. | Every authenticated heartbeat must re-read command delivery state immediately before responding and deliver pending update, diagnostics, window-action, and export commands without an additional offline gate or any client SQL-data change. | Heartbeat now performs one final scoped Agent read and explicitly marks its command payloads as an authenticated client poll; public status payloads retain the offline/waiting presentation. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_heartbeat_rereads_fresh_command_delivery_state_before_response` |
| INC-042 | A client received its forced update command and launched the detached updater, but remained on the old version indefinitely after that updater did not replace the running process. | Both Flutter update paths set their in-memory applying flag before launching a detached process and cleared it only on launch failure; an updater failure after launch permanently suppressed retries, while the supervisor skipped checks whenever the client was running. | A detached updater that does not replace the process must become retryable after a bounded cooldown, and the hidden supervisor must independently verify the manifest even while the client is healthy; updater mutexes must prevent concurrent installation. | Both Flutter update paths now clear their applying latch and retry after ten minutes, and the hidden supervisor runs its mutex-protected manifest updater every ten minutes regardless of agent health while still honoring an explicit user-close marker. | `tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_windows_agent_can_apply_server_requested_client_updates`<br>`tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_windows_agent_supervisor_runs_hidden_bounded_update_checks` |
| INC-043 | A complete Sync All took about nine minutes, while the web showed only 30-second or 1-minute per-job durations and hid the full user-observed time. | Each bounded four-table wave had independent job clocks, no durable operation clock spanned all waves, and completed slots waited for a later minute scheduler tick before the next table was queued. | Sync All must persist one start-to-finish clock across every bounded wave, display its live/last total in the client web UI, and refill a completed slot immediately without exceeding the existing four-table concurrency bound. | The control plane now stores durable Sync All operation timing, exposes it through one bounded live-state read, displays the total beside Sync All and per owner in the client list, and pipelines the pending queue by refilling one available slot when a table batch completes. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_sync_all_tracks_whole_operation_and_refills_bounded_slots`<br>`tests/test_control_plane_perf_contracts.py::test_live_state_preloads_sync_all_operations_in_one_bounded_read`<br>`frontend/test/sync_job_duration_test.dart::Sync All duration covers the complete durable operation` |

A later client/table heartbeat may make it eligible for a future Sync All. | Initial and deferred scheduling now classify no-participant/unavailable tables as ineligible for the current operation, remove them from the durable pending queue, reduce the operation table count, and close the durable clock when the last runnable batch is gone. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_sync_all_finishes_when_a_table_has_no_current_peer_target` |

Runtime status also checked pending client updates before active sync jobs and did not distinguish a retry from installation. | Ordinary automatic jobs must be change-driven and retain the configured interval only as a per-table eligibility/retry bound. Initial baselines, offline debt, manual Sync All, and explicit recovery remain schedulable. The web must prioritize real sync activity and label pending/retrying updates accurately. | The selector now rejects unchanged table states before interval evaluation and accepts only durable local-change or `baseline_pending` signals; existing offline/manual paths remain separate. Server and web status precedence now reports active sync first, then `Update pending` or `Update retrying`, so an idle caught-up client remains Ready instead of generating misleading no-op activity. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_periodic_scheduler_skips_unchanged_maintenance_tables`<br>`tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_client_status_prioritizes_sync_and_names_update_retries` |

| INC-075 | On a slow or interrupted connection, a Windows client could repeatedly restart a changed-file download from byte zero even though releases were already differential. | Every updater attempt used a new temporary payload directory, deleted incomplete output after transport errors, and the web artifact endpoint did not honor HTTP byte ranges. Completed unchanged files were skipped, but progress inside the current changed file was not durable across retries or updater restarts. | Updates must compare files individually, retain verified cached files, preserve incomplete bytes across process restarts, request only the missing byte range, verify final size and SHA-256 before atomic installation, and safely restart from zero when a server ignores range requests. | The updater now uses a version-scoped persistent cache and `.part` files, resumes with an HTTP Range request, retains partial bytes after bounded failures, and copies only verified files into the transactional staging area. The web endpoint advertises and serves bounded byte ranges with correct 206/416 headers; existing atomic install, rollback, and full-package fallback remain. | `tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_windows_update_resumes_verified_differential_payloads`<br>`tests/test_client_update_range.py::test_client_artifact_endpoint_serves_resumable_byte_ranges` |

| INC-076 | After alshallan2 was manually upgraded successfully to `1.0.254+258`, the clients page still retained `clientUpdateStatus=failed` from an older `1.0.253+257` manifest timeout even though authenticated heartbeats reported the newer executable. | A terminal update acknowledgement persisted its historical status, while later heartbeats updated only `clientVersion`; no reconciliation recognized that a non-pending failed/retrying request had been superseded by an actually changed installed version. | An authenticated heartbeat that reports a changed installed version must clear a stale non-pending failed/retrying update state, while a still-pending request must retain its delivery/retry semantics. | Heartbeat persistence now changes the status to `current` and records a confirmation message only when the reported executable version changed, the old state is failed/retrying, and no request remains pending. Both full-inventory and lightweight heartbeat writes apply the same reconciliation. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_heartbeat_clears_stale_update_failure_after_manual_upgrade` |

| INC-077 | A production image release stopped after all frontend layers were uploaded because the registry returned a transient EOF while committing the immutable manifest; the first deploy retry then stopped safely before mutation when registry manifest inspection timed out. | The repository-owned image builder and deployer treated the first nonzero registry operation as terminal even though manifest writes and reads can fail transiently after reusable layers and the immutable tag are already present. | Immutable backend/frontend pushes and deployment preflight manifest checks must retry a bounded number of times with backoff, preserve the exact commit tag, and still fail closed before cluster mutation after retry exhaustion. | The image builder now wraps each exact-tag push in three bounded attempts, and the deployer applies the same policy to both manifest inspections before any `kubectl set image`. Retries reuse content-addressed layers and the same immutable reference; no mutable tag or remote build fallback is introduced. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_production_image_push_retries_transient_registry_failures` |

| INC-078 | One-hour convergence monitoring found three enabled tables marked `Completed` with no pending Change Tracking rows even though client fingerprints differed: `Connections` had 9 vs 10 rows, `RepSrcs` had 32 vs 42 rows, and `ma000` had equal row counts but different content hashes. | The baseline planner already selected a safe full-client primary-key union when complete fingerprints differed, but the change-driven periodic selector only admitted local Change Tracking/baseline signals. A historical missed row or content difference with current cursors therefore never reached that planner and remained falsely “Up to date.” | Complete per-table fingerprint divergence across two or more online enabled clients must be a bounded automatic repair signal, use the existing insert/update-only full-client union, never infer deletion, respect retry intervals, and converge without creating ordinary unchanged-table waves. | The owner scheduler now compares complete reported fingerprints, adds at most one bounded wave of divergent tables, applies the existing per-table attempt cooldown, and feeds them to the established `union_bootstrap` planner. Normal identical tables remain change-driven; the union retains target-only rows and explicit-delete-only semantics. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_periodic_scheduler_repairs_complete_fingerprint_divergence`<br>`tests/docker-sync/run_scenarios.py::initial-three-client-primary-key-union-bootstrap`<br>`tests/docker-sync/run_scenarios.py::complete-reconcile-preserves-target-only-unicode-retry` |
| INC-079 | After fingerprint convergence, ordinary Change Tracking signals for `Connections` and `RepSrcs` caused full-table uploads (10 and 42 rows from each client) instead of uploading only the changed rows. | The shared planner evaluated expected pre-sync fingerprint divergence before checking that every participant had a valid cursor and a pending local change. A normal edit therefore selected `union_bootstrap` even though the safe delta path was available. | When every participant has a valid Change Tracking cursor and any participant reports pending local changes, process the delta first. Only use fingerprint-union repair after pending delta work is gone, so historical drift still converges without turning small edits into full uploads. | The planner now gives valid-cursor pending work an explicit delta branch before inventory-divergence union branches. If unrelated historical drift remains after the delta, INC-078's bounded detector schedules the insert/update-only union later. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_pending_change_with_valid_cursors_stays_on_delta_path`<br>`tests/docker-sync/run_scenarios.py::independent-multi-writer`<br>`tests/docker-sync/run_scenarios.py::large-1200-row-batch` |
| INC-080 | Live verification found six persistent divergent tables with no failed or active jobs. The oldest candidates reached their retry boundary, but later due tables were not scheduled because an earlier divergent candidate was still cooling down. | `divergent_fingerprint_tables_for_owner` stopped collecting after the scheduler's two-table execution limit, before `periodic_sync_table_due_after_attempt` filtered cooling-down candidates. Ineligible early candidates could therefore hide eligible later divergences and delay convergence indefinitely when an early table repeatedly diverged. | Evaluate retry eligibility across every reported divergent table, then apply ordering and the existing two-table execution bound only to eligible candidates. A cooling-down table must never consume a repair slot or starve another table. | The owner scheduler now requests the complete in-memory divergent candidate set, filters each candidate by its per-table cooldown, orders the eligible set, and retains the established final two-table queue limit. SQL/client work remains bounded while repair selection is starvation-free. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_periodic_scheduler_repairs_complete_fingerprint_divergence`<br>`tests/docker-sync/run_scenarios.py::complete-reconcile-preserves-target-only-unicode-retry`<br>`tests/docker-sync/run_scenarios.py::offline-peer-online-continuity-and-reconnect-catch-up` |
| INC-081 | A full-table repair over a slow, disconnecting WAN remained at 20% and could download the same already-received pages again after the Windows client restarted; an upload restart could also recreate its source snapshot while the server retained earlier chunk identities. | Protocol-v4 retried each HTTP call and deduplicated server upload chunks, but download pages and the exact upload snapshot existed only in process memory. There was no immutable transfer manifest, per-page content hash, durable cursor, or restart-stable upload document. | Every long transfer must use an immutable manifest and SHA-256 content identity, durably stage verified pages/snapshots on disk, resume after connection or process loss, expose page/row progress independently, retain atomic final SQL apply, and bound stale cache storage. | The server now emits content-addressed immutable transfer manifests and page metadata. The Windows client persists hash-verified download pages and the exact upload snapshot, replays verified pages after restart, resumes from the saved cursor, reports transfer progress, keeps the final SQL merge atomic, and removes completed/expired/over-quota cache data. | `sync_windows_agent/test/live_sync_api_test.dart::multi-writer download resumes verified pages after restart`<br>`sync_windows_agent/test/sync_transfer_cache_test.dart::corrupt cached page is discarded instead of applied`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_slow_network_sync_transfers_are_durable_and_content_verified` |
| INC-082 | The immutable backend build stopped before image publication because TRU rejected the new transfer payload ternary with `ternary expects matching branch types`. | The stored Base64 variable and `json.stringify` result are both runtime strings, but TRU inferred different static branch types and requires an exact type match for a ternary expression. | Transfer payload selection must compile under the authoritative TRU validation gate, preserve identical content hashing, and fail before registry or cluster mutation if syntax or types are invalid. | Replaced the ternary with a string-normalized mutable assignment and an explicit empty-payload branch. The immutable Docker compiler gate remains mandatory and the source regression forbids reintroducing the incompatible expression. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_transfer_payload_uses_tru_type_stable_assignment`<br>`backend/Dockerfile::TRU_VALIDATE_ONLY=1` |
| INC-083 | A local immutable image build printed both image references after the registry closed a frontend blob upload connection, but `docker manifest inspect` proved that the frontend tag did not exist. | On this Docker/registry path, `docker push` emitted a fatal copy error yet returned a success exit code to the PowerShell caller. Retry logic trusted only that exit code, so it did not retry; the separate deployer preflight still prevented cluster mutation. | Every push attempt must be considered successful only when the exact immutable registry manifest is readable. A missing manifest must trigger bounded backoff/retry and final failure before deployment regardless of the push process exit code. | The image builder now runs `docker manifest inspect` after every apparently successful push and feeds that result into the same three-attempt backoff. It prints release references only after both exact manifests exist; the deployer retains its independent preflight. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_production_image_push_retries_transient_registry_failures`<br>`scripts/deploy_production_images.ps1::Assert-RegistryManifestAvailable` |
| INC-084 | The live Alshallan2 update monitor exited during polling with Windows error 10054 even though its durable update request remained pending and heartbeats continued. | The verifier recognized connection-reset text as retryable, but `read_json_request` caught only `urllib.error.URLError`; Python can raise `ConnectionResetError` directly while reading an established TLS response, bypassing the retry classifier. | Live verification polling must retry bounded direct socket resets and timeouts without duplicating the already-durable update request or misreporting the client as failed. | The verifier now catches direct `ConnectionResetError` and `TimeoutError` alongside URL errors, passes them through the existing bounded retry policy, and preserves the same request/poll semantics. | `tests/test_live_verifier_scripts.py::test_client_update_read_retries_direct_windows_connection_reset` |
| INC-085 | Alshallan2 was online and heartbeating on `1.0.254+258`, but its durable request for `1.0.255+259` remained displayed as `requested`, so the web UI could not distinguish an accepted slow/interrupted download from a command that the client never received. Fresh remote diagnostics also omitted `update.log`, hiding the updater's exact phase. | The agent launched the updater without acknowledging a transfer phase. The server therefore retained the original generic status until the new executable restarted, while updater details existed only on the client PC. The payload transfer itself already used persistent hash-addressed `.part` files, but that durable progress was neither visible nor remotely diagnosable. | An online client must acknowledge the durable download phase before launching its updater while leaving the command pending until the target version heartbeats. Verified update files/partial bytes and verified sync pages must survive network/process loss, and remote diagnostics must include a bounded updater log tail. | The agent now acknowledges `downloading` with explicit resume semantics; the control plane treats `downloading`/`installing` as retryable pending phases; the clients page reports the exact update phase; diagnostics include a bounded `update.log` tail. The updater continues to reuse SHA-256-addressed files and HTTP Range `.part` files across invocations, while sync continues to reuse its verified page/snapshot cache. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_client_update_download_phase_is_durable_and_visible`<br>`tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_windows_update_resumes_verified_differential_payloads`<br>`tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_server_requested_update_reports_durable_download_phase`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_online_client_update_reports_and_resumes_durable_transfer` |
| INC-086 | Live monitoring showed Alshallan2's `mt000` job finish all 282 verified network pages and begin the long atomic SQL phase, but the web continued to report `downloading` at 38% with the last transfer message. | The agent set `applying` before starting the resumable download, then every page callback overwrote that state with `downloading`. After the final page was buffered, it entered SQL apply without restoring the applying state, so healthy atomic work appeared stalled in the network phase. | After the complete content-verified snapshot is buffered and before any SQL mutation starts, the durable job must report `applying` with an explicit atomic-apply message. A reconnect during download must still resume verified pages, and an interrupted apply must still roll back atomically. | The client now publishes a fresh `applying` progress transition immediately after verified buffering and before `_applyDownloadedSnapshotToTarget`. Existing transactional apply, rollback, SQLSYNC context, and durable transfer-cache behavior are unchanged. | `tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_verified_download_reports_applying_before_atomic_sql_work`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_completed_transfer_reports_atomic_apply_phase`<br>`tests/docker-sync/run_scenarios.py::rejected-row-rollback-and-recovery` |
| INC-087 | Review during the long live `mt000` apply found that publishing a newer client could let the outer shell update timer launch an updater while the authenticated Agent page was still inside a sync transaction. The inner Agent-page updater correctly waited for active jobs, but the shell path had no job visibility. | Two independent automatic-update paths existed. The shell path was designed for startup/login recovery and only checked update/retry state; it did not defer to the authenticated Agent page after login, so it could bypass `_processingJobIds` and active-job guards. | A routine client release must never intentionally interrupt an authenticated upload, verified download, or atomic SQL apply. Signed-out startup recovery may update immediately; once authenticated, only the job-aware Agent page may schedule installation, and network/power loss must remain recoverable through durable checkpoints and SQL rollback. | The shell updater now returns whenever an authenticated token is active. The Agent page remains the sole authenticated update owner and retains its active-job/row-loading guards. This prevents release-driven interruption while preserving signed-out repair and resumable recovery. | `tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_authenticated_shell_defers_updates_to_job_aware_agent_page`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_authenticated_update_waits_for_active_sync_work` |
| INC-088 | Alshallan2 kept a 14,030-row `mt000` download job in its local comparison/apply phase for more than an hour after all 282 network pages were verified, while the equivalent peer operation finished much sooner. | Whole-row comparison divided incoming primary keys into batches of 100 and launched a new `sqlcmd` process/query for every batch. A 14,030-row table therefore required about 141 sequential process starts, connections, query compilations, and result parses before the atomic stage/merge could begin; local SQL contention amplified that fixed overhead. | Large comparisons must stage all requested primary keys through one bounded SQL input script and perform one set-based join, while preserving canonical whole-row hashing, post-upload local-change protection, atomic merge/rollback, target-only rows, and explicit-tombstone-only deletion. | The client now loads primary keys into a uniquely named staging table in SQL Server's 1,000-row bounded statements, joins the target to the distinct staged keys in one `sqlcmd` process, parses the same canonical rows, and always drops the stage. This removes the process-per-100-keys bottleneck without changing merge or deletion semantics. | `sync_windows_agent/test/sql_sync_merge_test.dart::primary-key comparison uses one staged set-based SQL script`<br>`tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_large_content_comparison_uses_one_set_based_sqlcmd_launch`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_large_row_comparison_is_set_based_not_process_per_page` |
| INC-089 | On Alshallan2's slow, disconnecting link, a differential update remained in the download phase for tens of minutes even though the dominant changed `data/app.so` file was highly compressible. | Differential files were resumable and hash-verified but transferred as raw binaries; the 7.19 MB compiled payload compressed to 2.80 MB, so 61% of its WAN bytes were avoidable. | Large changed files must use a resumable compressed representation when it saves at least 10%, verify the compressed transfer independently, expand into transactional staging, verify the original file size/hash before install, and retain raw URLs for older updater compatibility. | The publisher now emits gzip sidecars and transfer metadata for beneficial files. New updaters resume the compressed content by SHA-256, expand it locally, then enforce the existing original-file verification and atomic rollback-safe installation; older clients continue using the unchanged raw URL. | `tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_windows_update_uses_resumable_verified_compressed_differentials`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_client_differential_updates_compress_large_changed_files` |
| INC-090 | After both live clients reached `1.0.259+263`, Velvet Factory uploads succeeded but downloads for `mc000`, `ms000`, `ac000`, and `mt000` failed before mutation with SQL Server error 209, such as `Ambiguous column name 'GUID'`. | The new set-based primary-key lookup correctly qualified its join keys, but the canonical whole-row projection was still built with bare column references. Joining the target table to the staged-key CTE made any shared key-column name ambiguous. | Every target column in a joined whole-row comparison must be qualified with the target-table alias; failure must remain before atomic apply, with no delete or partial mutation. | The comparison now passes `target_row.[column]` into the existing type-aware canonical encoder for every synchronized column, preserving one-process set-based performance and exact hashing without ambiguity. | `tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_set_based_comparison_qualifies_every_target_projection_column`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_set_based_target_hash_projection_is_alias_qualified` |
| INC-091 | Publishing a newer client while an older client was still downloading could replace files beneath the shared `packages/latest-package` URL, causing the older partial file to fail its final hash and restart. | The updater persisted verified byte ranges, but `latest.json` pointed through a mutable file manifest and mutable package alias; resumability therefore did not pin the selected release identity across later publications. | A client must select one immutable release manifest, download only content-addressed files from that versioned package, keep older versioned packages on persistent storage, resume byte ranges after any disconnect, and fail closed if manifest identity changes. | `latest.json` now points to the release's immutable versioned `files.json`; every file URL remains under that versioned package. The updater verifies manifest version and commit, publishing adds rather than replaces versioned packages, production images retain the current immutable package plus the legacy alias, and versioned artifacts receive immutable cache headers. | `tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_client_updates_pin_immutable_versioned_manifests_and_packages`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_interrupted_client_update_remains_on_its_immutable_release` |
| INC-092 | After its verified network download finished, Alshallan2 remained at 55% while applying 14,030 `mt000` rows for more than 30 minutes; diagnostics and three later tables were blocked, and the web had no finer progress signal. | INC-039 reduced staging to one `sqlcmd`, but that made the entire multi-statement stage load one opaque four-hour process. A disconnect or restart could not report or reuse committed staging batches, and the merge SQL deleted its staging table internally, preventing safe committed-response recovery. | Large apply staging must use a deterministic per-job table, load bounded committed chunks with server-visible progress, resume from the durable staged row count after process/network loss, leave the reusable stage immutable during merge, and mutate the target only in the existing atomic final transaction. | The client now creates or reuses a deterministic `tempdb` stage, queries its committed row count, inserts only missing 1,000-row chunks with progress from 55–80%, then copies the immutable stage into a transaction-local work table and reports the atomic merge at 85%. It drops the durable stage only after a confirmed successful merge, so response loss safely retries idempotently without reloading completed chunks. | `sync_windows_agent/test/sql_sync_merge_test.dart::resumable staging preserves an existing operation table and reports its row count`<br>`tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_atomic_stage_load_is_chunked_resumable_and_reports_progress`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_atomic_apply_staging_resumes_chunks_and_keeps_final_merge_atomic` |

| INC-093 | After INC-092 was deployed, the active 14,030-row `mt000` job could still remain at 55% before apply-stage progress began, and requested diagnostics remained queued while the client was busy. | Delta apply performs a whole-row target comparison before mutation. INC-088 made that comparison one set-based SQL command, but its primary-key input stage was still created and loaded inside one opaque process, so slow SQL compilation, interruption, or restart had no durable key-load checkpoint or progress signal. | Large pre-apply comparisons must load deterministic primary-key stages in bounded committed chunks, resume from the durable staged count for the immutable job, report progress separately from atomic apply staging, keep the target lookup set-based, and never mutate business rows during comparison. | The comparison now reuses a deterministic per-job `tempdb` key stage, reads its committed count, loads only missing 1,000-key chunks with progress from 55-65%, then performs one alias-qualified set-based target read. Successful or confirmed-invalid comparisons clean up the stage; process/transport interruption preserves it for retry. Apply staging now owns 65-80% and the final target merge remains one atomic transaction at 85%. | `sync_windows_agent/test/sql_sync_merge_test.dart::primary-key comparison can query a preloaded resumable key stage`<br>`tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_large_content_comparison_stages_resumable_bounded_key_chunks`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_large_hash_comparison_resumes_bounded_key_staging` |

| INC-094 | The clients web page could show only `Update downloading` for a slow or repeatedly disconnected Windows client, with no indication of bytes received or whether its resumable transfer was advancing. | The detached updater owned the HTTP byte stream, while the authenticated Flutter process only acknowledged a coarse phase before launch. No safe local telemetry bridge existed, and passing the client's server credential to PowerShell would have exposed authentication material. | Update downloads must persist an atomic local byte checkpoint, preserve resumable partial files, relay progress only through the authenticated client, and show percentage plus downloaded/total bytes on the clients page without allowing telemetry failure to interrupt installation. | The updater now writes throttled atomic `update-progress.json` checkpoints for differential and full-package transfers. The authenticated client validates the target version and relays bounded progress through the existing acknowledgement API; the control plane stores it and the clients table renders percentage and byte totals. Progress writes are best effort, credentials never enter the updater, and reconnects continue from existing `.part` files. | `tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_client_update_download_progress_is_checkpointed_and_relayed`<br>`tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_client_update_progress_is_persisted_and_exposed`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_resumable_client_update_progress_is_visible_without_credentials` |

| INC-095 | The frontend analyzer reported an unreachable switch case in the client activity color mapper. | `update retrying` appeared in both the active-update group and the waiting group, so the second branch could never execute and made the intended color contract ambiguous. | Every client activity state must have one deterministic color branch, and production frontend analysis must remain warning-free. | Removed the duplicate waiting-group case; retrying updates retain the existing blue active-update color through a single reachable branch. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_client_update_retrying_has_one_reachable_color_case`<br>`frontend/lib/clients_page.dart::_clientActivityColor` |

| INC-096 | The Windows rollback release gate could fail without restoring the previous client when the replacement executable was invalid and its supervisor exited during every bounded launch attempt. | Rollback was invoked only when the supervisor launched successfully and the later stability poll returned false. An exception thrown directly by `Start-UpdatedClient` escaped the finalizer before `Restore-InstallRollbackSnapshot` ran. | Any replacement launch failure or post-launch instability must enter the same transactional rollback path, restore and verify every prior managed file, restart the previous client, and leave the failed release unapplied. | Replacement launch and stability verification now form one guarded attempt. A launch exception is logged and converted to an unstable result, which always invokes the existing complete rollback snapshot restoration and previous-client health check. | `tests/test_windows_updater_rollback.ps1`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_updater_launch_failure_enters_transactional_rollback` |

| INC-097 | The clients page said Alshallan2 was applying `mt000`, while the bounded live-state job list reported zero active jobs. | The web history selected only the newest jobs by creation time. A long-lived job created before many later completed jobs could fall beyond the history limit even though a separate activity query and direct client queue still treated it as active. | Every active job must be present in the bounded web job payload regardless of creation age; recent terminal history may fill only the remaining slots, with job IDs deduplicated. | Live state now prioritizes the complete visible active-job set, then appends recent history without duplicate IDs before applying the configured response bound. Active rows use the full public job projection, so status, phase, rows, timing, and errors stay coherent everywhere. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_live_state_prioritizes_active_jobs_before_bounded_history`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_active_sync_job_cannot_disappear_from_bounded_web_history` |

| INC-098 | A client could show only a coarse phase such as `Applying`, leaving operators unable to tell its percentage, current transfer/apply rate, or whether progress had stopped. | Job progress existed in the API, but the clients-table status reduced active jobs to a phase label and discarded progress checkpoints between the page's 15-second refreshes. | Every active client phase must show bounded job percentage and a measured rows/second or percent/minute rate when progress moves; a nonmoving phase must show its elapsed no-movement time, while queued/waiting states must not invent a speed. | The web now prioritizes the client's real active job, renders phase plus percentage, retains per-job checkpoint samples across refreshes, computes rate from monotonic row/progress deltas, and reports prolonged no-movement time. Samples are pruned when jobs finish, so old rates cannot leak into later work. | `tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_clients_page_shows_active_phase_progress_and_speed`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_active_client_status_reports_progress_rate_or_stall` |

| INC-099 | Alshallan2 staged a 14,030-row `mt000` apply at about 125 rows/minute even after its content-verified download had completed; every 1,000 rows required another `sqlcmd` process, connection, compilation, and a large literal `INSERT`. | Resumable staging correctly bounded commits after INC-092/093, but implemented each checkpoint as expanded SQL text. Wide rows multiplied parsing and process overhead, and the same path also slowed the primary-key comparison stage. | Comparison and apply staging must use SQL Server's typed bulk-copy protocol with bounded durable transactions, retain deterministic stage tables and progress, re-read committed row count after interrupted responses, and fall back safely for incompatible Windows/SQL installations without weakening the final atomic merge or deletion rules. | Windows clients now load typed rows through a hidden .NET `SqlBulkCopy` helper in bounded 10,000-row invocations with 1,000-row internal transactions. Both comparison and apply stages re-read the durable identity count after any bulk failure before using the literal-SQL compatibility fallback. The final merge remains the existing single atomic transaction. A representative 14,030-row Docker benchmark improved from 6.16s to 1.74s (3.54x) while verifying every row. | `sync_windows_agent/test/sql_bulk_stage_test.dart::bulk request preserves column order and excludes computed columns`<br>`tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_windows_snapshot_staging_uses_resumable_sql_bulk_copy`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_slow_literal_staging_uses_bounded_resumable_bulk_copy`<br>`tests/benchmark_sql_bulk_stage.ps1` |

| INC-100 | The immutable production frontend image build failed after the Flutter tool's web-SDK connection closed before HTTP headers completed; the backend image had built, but no image push or deployment occurred. | The container build allowed `flutter build web` to perform its first web-SDK download with no bounded retry, and the outer local image builder treated the first frontend build failure as terminal even though Docker layers and the immutable target were safe to reuse. | Flutter web dependencies and the exact immutable frontend image build must tolerate bounded transient network failures, reuse Docker cache, verify the resulting local image, and still fail closed without deployment after retry exhaustion. | The frontend Dockerfile now precaches the web SDK with three bounded attempts before the application build. The repository image builder also retries the exact frontend image build up to three times with backoff and verifies the local immutable image after success; it never changes the tag or falls back to a remote build. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_frontend_image_retries_transient_flutter_web_sdk_downloads` |

| INC-101 | Velvet Factory downloaded and verified a newer client, but remained on the old version while its large `mt000` apply repeatedly resumed; the web stayed at `Update: installing`. | A server-requested update called the automatic updater with `force=true`, which bypassed the active-job guard. The updater could stop the executable during resumable SQL staging; the SQL transaction stayed safe and the job resumed, but update and sync could starve each other. | A delivered update must remain durable and take priority over the next queued job, but it must never terminate a process that owns an active SQL operation. Installation must launch immediately at the first clean job boundary. | Forced updates now use the same active-operation guard, retain a pending forced-update marker, stop taking additional queued jobs after the current job finishes, and retry installation only after the processing loop releases its safe boundary. | `tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_server_requested_update_waits_for_active_sql_job_boundary`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_server_update_cannot_interrupt_active_sql_apply` |

| INC-106 | Velvet Factory stopped during the 1.0.271 install but immediately returned on 1.0.268. | The updater starts supervisors with `-EncodedCommand`, but its process discovery searched only the literal PowerShell command line for the supervisor path. | INC-110 | Velvet Factory remained at a verified 100% install checkpoint, but no Windows administrator prompt appeared after the update was requested again. | The initial install-directory probe succeeded even though the later replacement handoff could not complete. | INC-113 | The updater installed and hash-verified version 1.0.276, but the new supervisor exited ten times and forced rollback while another supervisor still owned the install mutex. | The startup gate judged only whether the newly launched supervisor process survived for 500 ms.
