| INC-114 | Elevated updater cleanup logged errors for many unrelated Windows scheduled tasks because some task actions did not expose an `Execute` property. | Under strict PowerShell mode the cleanup assumed every scheduled-task action was an executable action, but Windows also returns other action types with different property sets. | Scoped registration cleanup must inspect action capabilities, skip non-executable action types, and never disable or emit per-task errors for unrelated Windows tasks. | Updater and supervisor task inspection now read action properties through `PSObject.Properties`, include only actions that expose `Execute`, and preserve narrow SQL Sync path matching. | `tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_windows_task_cleanup_skips_non_executable_action_types`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_non_exec_scheduled_task_actions_do_not_break_scoped_cleanup` |
| INC-115 | The Standard verification gate intermittently failed because the isolated supervisor fixture exited or did not log incomplete-install suppression. | The disposable script used the production name `sync_windows_agent_supervisor.ps1`, so a real local supervisor could correctly classify and terminate it as an obsolete installation; the fixture also assumed a fixed two-second CIM startup latency. | A disposable fixture must not match production retirement scope and must poll its observable outcome with a bounded deadline while failing immediately on genuine process exit. | The fixture now uses the non-production name `isolated_supervisor_fixture.ps1` and polls its log every 200 ms for up to 15 seconds while checking process health. | `tests/test_windows_supervisor.ps1`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_incomplete_install_fixture_waits_for_agent_validation` |
| INC-116 | After the fixed 1.0.277 release was published, Velvet Factory reported the old `Stop-ObsoleteInstallProcesses is not recognized` failure again. | The release manifest pointed every version at the mutable `/client/update.ps1`; Windows or an intermediary cache reused an older successful response even though the live file had been replaced. | Every client release must identify its updater with an immutable version/commit-scoped URL so cached updater code can never cross release boundaries. | The publisher now writes `updateScriptUrl` beneath the immutable package directory (`/client/packages/<version-build-commit>/update.ps1`), while retaining the top-level updater only as a compatibility artifact. | `tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_client_manifest_uses_immutable_release_scoped_updater`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_client_updater_url_is_immutable_per_release` |
| INC-117 | The focused contract run failed with `NameError: update_script is not defined` after immutable-updater coverage was added. | The new test method was inserted before the final assertions of the preceding resumable-download test, moving those assertions into a scope without its local fixtures. | Contract additions must preserve complete existing test-method boundaries and the full suite must pass before artifact publication. | The resumable-download assertions were restored to their original method and the immutable-updater test now contains only its publisher-specific assertions. | `tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_windows_update_resumes_verified_differential_payloads`<br>`tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_client_manifest_uses_immutable_release_scoped_updater` |
| INC-118 | Both exact-commit production images finished building locally, then the first registry push failed with `authorization failed: no basic auth credentials`, wasting the complete build time while safely leaving production unchanged. | The production image builder checked registry availability only after each push; it had no read-access/authentication preflight before creating contexts and compiling the images. | A push-enabled production build must prove that this Docker client can access both application repositories before any archive or image build starts, fail with the exact `docker login` prerequisite when access is missing, and never deploy after a failed preflight. | The image builder now inspects a known tag in both backend and frontend repositories before creating build contexts whenever push is enabled. Missing access fails immediately with the registry login command; `-SkipPush` remains available for intentional local-only builds. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_production_image_build_fails_fast_without_registry_access` |
| INC-119 | Registry authentication succeeded, but the production preflight still failed because it assumed a mutable `dev` tag existed while this registry retained only immutable release tags. | The access probe conflated repository authorization with one hard-coded mutable tag convention that the deployment rules explicitly forbid. | Registry access checks must use an explicitly supplied known existing immutable tag and must never infer that `dev` or `latest` exists. Missing probe identity must fail before building with an actionable message. | The push-enabled builder now requires `RegistryAccessProbeTag` to be supplied explicitly, rejects an empty probe before creating build contexts, and retains local-only `-SkipPush` behavior without a registry requirement. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_production_image_build_fails_fast_without_registry_access` |
| INC-120 | Alshallan2 reconnected on `1.0.267+271`, but its completed update state still referred to superseded target `1.0.268+272`; the server left it failed instead of resolving the current immutable `1.0.277+281` release. | Update requests persisted an exact target version, but a terminal stale request had no owner-scoped release reference or automatic heartbeat retargeting after a newer client successfully confirmed a later release. | The newest successfully acknowledged client release within an owner must become the propagation reference. An older heartbeat with no active request must receive an untargeted manifest-resolving request; terminal retries must be cooldown-bounded, offline delivery must remain durable, and newer/equal clients must never be downgraded or loop. | Heartbeat now selects the most recently acknowledged `current` client release for the owner, compares acknowledgement ordering for clients already marked current, and atomically replaces eligible stale requests with an untargeted request that resolves the live immutable manifest. Failed/retrying requests wait ten minutes before another attempt. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_heartbeat_retargets_outdated_clients_to_latest_confirmed_release`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_outdated_client_reconnect_retargets_current_immutable_release` |
| INC-121 | `ce000` repeatedly transferred its complete merged snapshot even though each guarded union completed successfully; Alshallan2 retained one protected target-only row, so its fingerprint remained different from Velvet Factory. | The scheduler defined `latest_completed_table_batch_was_union` but never used it when admitting fingerprint-repair candidates. A difference that a safe insert/update-only union intentionally could not erase was therefore scheduled again after every interval. | One automatic complete union may repair historical divergence, but the same unchanged protected divergence must not trigger another full-table union. A later explicit Change Tracking edit must still run as a delta, after which one further bounded union may retry genuine remaining historical drift. Manual Sync All must remain explicit and deletion must still require a tombstone. | Automatic fingerprint repair now skips a table whose latest completed upload batch was already a union. Change Tracking candidates are selected before this guard, so real later edits remain incremental and make a subsequent bounded repair eligible without weakening target-only-row or explicit-delete safety. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_completed_union_suppresses_unchanged_automatic_full_table_loop`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_protected_divergence_does_not_repeat_full_union_automatically` |
| INC-122 | A client was stopped and relaunched about every twelve minutes for nearly two days while every verified update installed, failed its startup gate, rolled back, and retried. | The supervisor synchronously waited for the updater while retaining the install-scoped mutex. Supervisor shutdown used best-effort process enumeration with suppressed stop errors and no exit verification, so the exact launcher could remain alive, block the replacement supervisor, and make a healthy payload look unable to start. | The supervisor that directly launches an updater must be identified by PID, stopped and confirmed exited before replacement startup; broad shutdown must also fail closed when any target supervisor survives. Manual updater launches must never terminate their PowerShell console, elevation retry and transactional rollback must remain intact, and future retries must not form a restart loop. | The supervisor now passes its exact PID to the updater, while direct launches safely discover a parent only when its decoded command line names the target supervisor. Both parent and elevated deferred stages validate and stop that exact launcher, wait for target supervisors to disappear, and escalate or roll back instead of silently continuing while the mutex is owned. | `tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_updater_uses_exact_launcher_pid_and_verified_supervisor_shutdown`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_updater_stops_exact_launcher_supervisor_before_mutex_handoff` |
| INC-123 | Clicking Resume Automatic Sync could report success while scheduled checks remained paused. | An owner-scoped resume returned the requested false state without checking the effective administrator-level global pause, so the UI could display a false success even though the scheduler correctly remained stopped. | An owner must never override an administrator pause or receive a successful resume response while that global pause remains. The API must return an actionable conflict before mutating owner state; an administrator resume continues to clear global and owner pause markers. | Owner resume now fails with HTTP conflict semantics and a clear administrator-action message whenever the global pause is active. The guard runs before owner-state mutation, while the existing admin path remains the only path that clears the global marker. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_automatic_sync_pause_is_admin_controlled_and_persistent`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_owner_resume_cannot_misreport_global_admin_pause` |
| INC-124 | The first exact-commit production build after moving the backend submodule to its current master stopped because the backend Dockerfile could not find `tru.json`. | The standalone backend image contract expects its configuration at context root, while the parent production builder archived the project configuration only at `business/tru.json`; updating the submodule exposed this unadapted context boundary. | Parent production builds must always use the tracked root `business/tru.json` as the application configuration and adapt it to the standalone backend Dockerfile's context filename before any image build or push. A missing tracked configuration must fail before Docker compilation. | The production image builder now verifies the archived root business configuration and copies that exact tracked file to backend-context `tru.json` before invoking Docker. The failed attempt pushed no image and changed no workload. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_production_backend_context_uses_root_business_config` |
| INC-125 | The clients page reported 2,127 downloaded rows for each client after a `ce000` union/bootstrap batch even though the clients uploaded only 27 and 28 accepted changes. | Complete canonical snapshots bypassed target-content comparison before apply. The client staged every canonical row and counted every matched row as updated, so transfer/verification size was mislabeled as actual row changes and unchanged rows were needlessly rewritten. | Last and total downloaded-row metrics must count only rows actually inserted, content-updated, or explicitly deleted. Complete union/bootstrap snapshots must still verify every canonical row, preserve target-only rows, and never infer deletion, but byte-identical rows must be skipped before the atomic merge. | All snapshot types now pass through the resumable canonical SHA-256 target comparison before staging. Only differing rows enter the atomic merge and its completion count, while the existing complete-snapshot verification still checks the full canonical row set afterward. | `tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_complete_snapshot_counts_only_content_changes`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_complete_snapshot_download_metric_excludes_unchanged_rows`<br>`tests/run_sync_verification.ps1` |
| INC-126 | Fresh log requests reached both healthy clients and remained pending for minutes even though they had zero failed tables and zero active jobs. | Diagnostic capture awaited a complete physical fingerprint refresh across every selected table before uploading the already-available retained logs and health summary. Hundreds of tables or large tables therefore made remote log retrieval appear stuck. | A diagnostic request must publish retained logs and current health immediately, then enrich the same request asynchronously with fresh complete fingerprints. A slow older enrichment must never overwrite a newer diagnostic request. | The client now uploads a bounded cached-fingerprint/log payload first with an explicit `refreshing` fingerprint state, marks the request uploaded, and runs complete physical fingerprint enrichment in the background. The enriched upload is request-ID guarded so newer requests win, and enrichment failure leaves the initial diagnostic intact. | `tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_diagnostics_upload_logs_before_full_fingerprint_enrichment`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_client_log_request_is_not_blocked_by_complete_fingerprint_scan` |
| INC-127 | Diagnostics from both 562-table clients reported uploaded, but their stored payload was exactly 64,000 characters and ended mid-JSON, so detailed logs could not be parsed. | The emergency diagnostics encoder preserved every verbose fingerprint object plus large log tails and returned without rechecking its final size. The server correctly enforced its 64,000-character field by truncating the oversized string. | Every diagnostic payload must be valid JSON within the client’s 60,000-character safety budget. It must compact complete fingerprints, prioritize the newest retained logs, explicitly report any omitted fingerprints, and never rely on server-side truncation. | Emergency diagnostics now encode fingerprints as a deterministic table-to-count/checksum map, retain only database access problems, dynamically allocate the remaining JSON-safe budget to log tails, recheck the encoded size after every reduction, and fall back to an explicit minimal valid document. | `tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_diagnostics_emergency_payload_is_bounded_valid_json`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_large_client_diagnostics_cannot_be_truncated_mid_json` |
| INC-128 | Remote diagnostics required reading raw JSON to understand client health, exposed authenticated account identity fields inside the uploaded payload, and gave the web administrator no stage progress while long fingerprint scans ran. | The original log-request contract was a single final upload designed for manual troubleshooting. It had no server-persisted stage/progress fields, no automated check model or unified trace, and included account display fields that were unnecessary for diagnosis. | Every support operation must be initiated and monitored from the web server over the authenticated heartbeat channel, run safe client self-tests automatically, expose bounded stage progress, provide a correlation timeline and downloadable sanitized report, and never upload credentials or account identity. | The server now persists diagnostic stage, percentage, start, and completion state and accepts authenticated client progress updates. A remote request makes the client run connectivity, SQL access, table, Change Tracking, and retained-log checks, upload a bounded redacted support report with a unified timeline, refresh fingerprints in the background, and finish at 100%. The web UI can run one or all self-tests, display progress and results, and download the report; account identity is excluded. | `tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_remote_support_self_test_is_server_driven_sanitized_and_staged`<br>`tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_diagnostics_progress_contract_is_authenticated_and_bounded`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_remote_support_report_excludes_account_identity_and_has_progress`<br>`sync_windows_agent/test/remote_support_diagnostics_test.dart`<br>`frontend/test/remote_support_diagnostics_test.dart` |
| INC-129 | The first backend rollout for the remote self-test release created an `ErrImagePull` pod while the previous healthy backend remained available. | The backend pod template uses the immutable backend image in both the main `backend` container and the `backend-data-permissions` init container, but the deploy helper updated only the main container. The init container retained an expired historical tag that was no longer present in the registry. | Every backend rollout must atomically pin both the main backend container and every backend-image init container to the same verified immutable commit before waiting for readiness. | The production deploy helper now updates `backend` and `backend-data-permissions` together in one `kubectl set image` operation. The live rollout was repaired with both references pinned to the release image, preserving availability through the failed attempt. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_backend_rollout_pins_init_and_main_containers_to_same_release` |
| INC-130 | The exact-commit backend rollout completed, but the deploy helper stopped before frontend and health verification when the SSH connection reset during a subsequent `kubectl rollout status` call. | Each remote Kubernetes command opened a new SSH connection without keepalives or bounded retry, so a transient control-channel reset turned an otherwise healthy idempotent rollout verification into a terminal deployment error. | Namespace-scoped immutable image updates and rollout checks must tolerate bounded transient SSH disconnects, preserve exact image references, and still fail closed after retry exhaustion. | Remote `kubectl` calls now use SSH keepalives and three bounded exponential-backoff attempts. Image updates are idempotent and remain exact-commit scoped; persistent failures still stop before a success claim. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_production_rollout_retries_transient_ssh_disconnects` |
| INC-131 | Live production verification helpers contained a default administrator username and password in tracked source. | The original operational scripts optimized for unattended invocation by embedding credentials instead of requiring secret injection from the operator environment. | Repository scripts must never contain production authentication credentials. Server automation must receive secrets through protected environment variables or explicit runtime arguments and fail before network access when they are absent. | Every `verify_live_*` helper now reads optional `SQL_SYNC_ADMIN_USERNAME` and `SQL_SYNC_ADMIN_PASSWORD` environment variables, still supports explicit arguments, and exits with an actionable parser error when either credential is missing; all tracked live-verification credential defaults were removed. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_live_update_verifier_never_embeds_admin_credentials` |
| INC-132 | Live client-state verification falsely failed both healthy current clients by expecting obsolete version `1.0.105+109`, even though both reported the published `1.0.282+286`. | The helper carried a historical hard-coded expected version instead of deriving the current immutable release from the server manifest. | When no explicit expected version is supplied, live verification must resolve `/client/latest.json`, require a non-empty version, and compare clients against that current published release. | The client-state verifier now defaults the option to empty, fetches and validates the live manifest version, and retains an explicit `--expected-version` override for release-specific checks. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_live_client_state_resolves_current_manifest_version` |
| INC-133 | During the August 20, 2026 live-server review, the node filesystem was 94% used (405 GiB of 435 GiB) and kubelet reported `FreeDiskSpaceFailed` 703 times over 2 days 10 hours while attempting to reclaim about 61 GiB; the node remained ready and had not yet entered `DiskPressure`. | Production omitted `TRU_HISTORY_MODE`, so the backend used unbounded default `full` audit history with no retention/pruning. The nominal 10 GiB local-path PostgreSQL PVC did not enforce capacity: the database used 64 GiB and `public.tru_history` used about 63 GiB for approximately 3.55 million rows. Two clients poll every 15 seconds while the server persists after 10 seconds, so almost every `Agent` heartbeat stores the complete large canonical row before, after, and again inside the audit envelope even though recent samples changed only `lastHeartbeat`, `updatedAt`, and relationship metadata. Relationship normalization additionally assigns `updatedAt: now_iso()` on every request, guaranteeing a false relationship change; unchanged table inventory and diagnostic fields dominate each row. This produced about 25 GiB of `Agent` history and was still adding roughly 0.95-1.1 GiB/day. The legacy upload path also appended each Base64 chunk to a growing `UploadSession.chunks` array and fully audited every intermediate version, causing quadratic amplification and about 20 GiB before those writes stopped on July 17. Kubernetes images were only a few GiB, so kubelet correctly found no eligible image space to reclaim; another roughly 2.5 GiB of heartbeat history since August 18 pushed the shared filesystem across the image-GC threshold. | Audit history growth and node free space must be bounded and observable without silently discarding required recovery/audit data. Heartbeat liveness must not fully audit unchanged inventories, relationship normalization must be stable, chunk transport must not audit growing payload aggregates, and the approved policy must define retention or archival semantics while preserving required replay guarantees. Production storage must alert before kubelet image-GC pressure and enforce or intentionally expand to its declared capacity. | The runtime now supports targeted class-level `history minimal`, used only for Agent, UploadSession, and DownloadSession operational state while unmarked canonical classes retain full history. Minimal rows preserve a stable identity (`id` or `clientName`) plus audit metadata without copying large payloads. Heartbeat relationship normalization no longer invents a fresh timestamp for unchanged client payloads. A secret-backed daily maintenance CronJob enforces the approved 7-day/100,000-entry bound and vacuums deleted history. The 3.55-million-row history was preserved in a validated 41,321,128,429-byte custom archive (SHA-256 `F0E751205448F820007231827586B3DAFC621AF549EA704CEB5E3740FF50D694`) plus a validated 33,890-row overlapping delta archive (SHA-256 `321294ACF3C2A117502003CB4EF498D504E49AA69178AEC38B3E5C825645BD00`) before truncating only `tru_history` without restarting its sequence. Node free space increased from 30 GiB to 96 GiB; no client database was used for reproduction or cleanup. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_unbounded_history_disk_incident_has_bounded_regression_fix`<br>`backend/server/tests/production_readiness_integration_tests.rs::class_level_minimal_history_compacts_high_churn_rows_without_weakening_canonical_audit`<br>`tests/test_heartbeat_contracts.py::test_relationship_timestamps_are_stable_when_client_omits_them`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_history_maintenance_is_bounded_secret_backed_and_scheduled` |
| INC-134 | The mandatory Standard verification gate stopped before Docker coverage because 31 live-verifier unit tests exited during argument parsing with `administrator credentials are required`. | INC-131 correctly removed embedded production credentials from every verifier, but the isolated unit harness still invoked verifier `main()` methods without explicit arguments or fake runtime credentials. The tests accidentally depended on the removed unsafe defaults. | Live-verifier tests must inject non-production credentials explicitly, while production scripts must continue to fail before network access when runtime credentials are absent. Test fixtures must never restore embedded live credentials. | The shared verifier test setup now supplies clearly isolated fake credentials through the same two environment variables used at runtime and removes them after every test. Production verifier code remains unchanged and credential-free. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_live_verifier_unit_harness_injects_fake_runtime_credentials`<br>`tests/test_live_verifier_scripts.py::def setUp(self):` |
| INC-135 | After isolated credentials were supplied, the client-state commit-mismatch unit test failed before reaching its intended assertion because its mocked manifest had no version. | INC-132 changed the helper to resolve the live manifest whenever `--expected-version` is omitted, but this older commit-only fixture neither mocked a valid manifest nor supplied the version that was irrelevant to its scenario. The credential failure had previously masked the stale fixture. | A commit-mismatch regression must provide every unrelated prerequisite explicitly so it tests the commit gate without making network/manifest behavior part of the fixture. | The test now passes an explicit non-production expected version and continues to assert the dedicated commit-mismatch exit code and message. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_client_state_commit_fixture_supplies_unrelated_version_prerequisite`<br>`tests/test_live_verifier_scripts.py::test_clients_state_main_returns_commit_mismatch_code` |
| INC-136 | The Standard verification child completed every requested step successfully and wrote `task-status.json` with `status=passed`, but the outer launcher still threw `Local test standard failed with exit code .`. | PowerShell's `Start-Process -PassThru` process object was not refreshed after `Wait-Process`; its cached `ExitCode` remained null even though the hidden child had exited normally. | The supported hidden launcher must refresh the completed process state before evaluating its exit code and must agree with the retained machine-readable task result. | The launcher now calls `Refresh()` immediately after `Wait-Process` and before reading `ExitCode`, preserving hidden execution and the existing result artifacts. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_hidden_standard_launcher_refreshes_exit_code_after_wait` |

| INC-137 | Four runner-bootstrap regressions failed on Windows with the Microsoft Store `python3` application alias even though a working Python 3 interpreter was available as `python`; dry-run start/stop and repository-state-path validation exited with code 49 before exercising their contracts. | The bootstrap selected `python3` by command-name presence only. Git Bash's `command -v` accepted the nonfunctional Windows application alias, and the script never validated that it could execute Python or fall back to the working `python` command. | Cross-platform runner setup must select an actually executable supported Python 3 interpreter, prefer a valid `python3`, fall back to a valid `python`, and emit one explicit prerequisite error only when neither works. | The original bootstrap added an executable Python probe and fallback. The later Cloud Tests migration removed that Windows runner entirely; current coverage proves the obsolete entry point stays absent and the Linux worker image installs Python 3 while each task fails explicitly when `python3` is unavailable. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_retired_windows_runner_python_alias_bug_cannot_reenter_cloud_tests` |

| INC-138 | After the runner bootstrap correctly fell back to Windows `python`, dry-run paths changed from Git Bash form to `C:/...`, and the repository-local state-directory safety regression stopped rejecting a path inside the checkout. | MSYS path conversion and host-native Python `os.path` semantics were mixed with shell paths: Python reinterpreted `/tmp`, and its Windows `commonpath` result was compared with a POSIX-normalized root. The nonfunctional `python3` alias had masked both failures. | Shell-facing runner paths must retain the active shell's canonical semantics on Linux and Git Bash, test inputs must not be silently rewritten by MSYS process launch, and repository-local state directories must remain rejected by a path-boundary check. | The bootstrap normalized shell paths consistently and rejected repository-local state. The later Cloud Tests migration removed the Windows runner and its host state directory entirely; current regression coverage proves that obsolete entry point remains absent and cannot reintroduce the mixed Windows/POSIX boundary. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_retired_windows_runner_python_alias_bug_cannot_reenter_cloud_tests` |

| INC-139 | The first backend submodule push for the production fix was rejected as non-fast-forward because `origin/master` had advanced by one independent commit. | The local submodule checkout was based on the previously deployed revision and the production image workflow had no explicit pre-build proof that both the root commit and referenced backend commit were present on their respective remote master branches. Git safely prevented overwriting remote history. | Production builds must use commits that are already reachable from the current remote master branches. Remote drift must be integrated with a normal fetch/rebase or merge; deployment tooling must fail before image construction and must never recommend a force-push. | The incident commit was rebased cleanly onto the independent remote change and pushed normally. The production builder now fetches `origin/master` for both repositories and requires the exact root and backend commits to be ancestors before any registry preflight or image build; its failure text explicitly forbids force-pushing. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_production_builder_requires_root_and_submodule_commits_on_remote` |

| INC-140 | The first production registry preflight checked a guessed full SHA that shared the deployed commit's short prefix but was not the actual immutable image tag, so manifest inspection failed before any build. During diagnosis, a local `docker logout` was also mistakenly run, temporarily removing this workstation's private-registry credential. No image or deployment was changed. | The operator used an incorrectly expanded commit prefix instead of reading the exact live deployment image. The preflight error attributed every manifest-inspection failure only to authentication, obscuring the equally valid missing-tag cause; the diagnostic command then included an unnecessary state-changing logout. | Registry diagnostics must remain read-only, use the exact immutable tag reported by the live deployment, and distinguish tag/reference errors from authentication prerequisites. Production scripts must never log out as part of a build or deployment check. | The local Docker credential was restored non-interactively from the namespace's existing `regcred` pull secret without printing it, and the exact deployed tag was read from `sql-sync-back`. The builder error now requires checking both the exact probe tag and authentication. A contract scans the production build/deploy scripts to prohibit `docker logout` and pins the tag-aware diagnostic. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_registry_probe_diagnostics_are_non_destructive_and_tag_aware` |

| INC-141 | The exact-commit Standard gate passed all eight steps in 515 seconds and wrote `task-status.json` with `status=passed`, but the supported outer launcher again threw `Local test standard failed with exit code .`. | `Wait-Process` waited by process ID in a separate cmdlet; refreshing the original `System.Diagnostics.Process` object afterward still did not populate its nullable `ExitCode` reliably. The prior INC-136 static regression proved call ordering but did not execute the Windows process-object behavior. | The hidden launcher must wait on the same process object returned by `Start-Process -PassThru`, obtain a concrete exit code, and agree with the retained task status on real Windows runs. The regression must execute a hidden child with a known nonzero code rather than checking source text alone. | The launcher now calls `$childProcess.WaitForExit()` on the original object, refreshes it, and then reads `ExitCode`. A Windows executable regression starts a hidden child that exits 7 and proves the wrapper API observes exactly 7; the source contract keeps the same wait/refresh/read ordering. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_hidden_process_waitfor_exit_returns_concrete_exit_code`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_hidden_standard_launcher_refreshes_exit_code_after_wait` |

| INC-142 | Helm revision 186 created the maintenance CronJob and updated the frontend, but the new backend pod entered `CrashLoopBackOff` with `Invalid TRU_EXECUTION_MEMORY_MAX_BYTES value ''`; the previous ready backend pod remained serving, so public availability was preserved. The attempted rollback then rendered an obsolete unavailable image from historical Helm values while the still-ready manually deployed pod remained in place. | The live release predated `backend.env.truExecutionMemoryMaxBytes`. With `--reuse-values`, Helm retained the older nested `backend.env` map instead of coalescing the new key, and the template rendered the missing value as an empty string. The chart contract checked only the default values file, not rendering under an older reused-values shape. | A missing or explicitly empty historical value must render the safe production default, Helm upgrades must supply the exact immutable images and required runtime limits explicitly, and rollout failure must leave the prior ready replica available. | The backend template now applies `default "536870912"` at render time, and the production override explicitly supplies the same value. The failed upgrade/rollback was allowed to settle while the prior healthy pod served traffic; the corrected chart is deployed only after lint/template and a regression pin the reuse-values fallback. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_backend_memory_limit_survives_helm_reuse_values`<br>`deployment/chart/tests/test_chart_contracts.py::test_backend_sets_memory_cap_below_pod_limit` |

| INC-143 | The first account-ledger screenshot showed a 1,329,000 difference that correlated exactly with changes in `ac000.Debit`, `Credit`, and `UseFlag`. The initial mitigation classified those columns as local caches, but paired post-release trial-balance screenshots still differed by the same balanced amount. | The initial investigation treated correlation as causation before verifying the complete `ce000` parent/header chain for the 1,329,000 `en000` detail. Excluding three schema-discovered columns was therefore an unsupported semantic assumption and prevented the requirement that all writable database data synchronize. | No application column may be excluded merely because its values resemble aggregates. Every writable column remains schema-discovered and synchronized unless vendor documentation or a controlled isolated experiment proves a different contract; relational parents and durable row history must be checked before changing column policy. | Removed the `ac000` column exclusion from upload, download/apply, and fingerprint paths and restored normal synchronization of `Debit`, `Credit`, `UseFlag`, and every other writable column. The paired-image evidence was traced to the durable-delete convergence defect documented and fixed in INC-144. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_ac000_columns_remain_schema_discovered_and_synchronized`<br>`sync_windows_agent/test/sql_sync_schema_test.dart::computed and rowversion columns are not writable sync columns` |

| INC-144 | Paired live trial-balance images showed Velvet Factory at 18,154,000/70,441,000 and Alshallan2 at 19,483,000/71,770,000 for accounts `121006` and `41`, an exactly balanced 1,329,000 divergence. Both clients uploaded the identical `en000` detail GUID `CE21B39F-07D0-4015-B4AB-1D95F1E45563`, but only Alshallan2 retained its `ce000` parent GUID `F1ED9BF3-27DA-4DD4-8588-D56504693394`. | Alshallan2 explicitly deleted that header on 12 August and the durable winner correctly remained operation `D`. A historical full merge later delivered an older snapshot `S` row back to Alshallan2, recreating a zombie header. Current union logic correctly refused to let that snapshot override the tombstone, but silently discarded the stale row instead of relaying the already-durable explicit tombstone back to the client that still reported it. Velvet therefore stayed deleted while Alshallan2 continued including the zombie header in reports. | Snapshot absence must never infer deletion, and a full snapshot must never resurrect a durable tombstone. When a client positively reports a row whose exact primary key is already covered by a durable explicit `D`, the server must reassert that same tombstone to every participant with its original durable identity and ordering metadata; client application must atomically interpret the reported snapshot row as `D`. | Full-union upload now recognizes a reported zombie row covered by a durable tombstone, retains the tombstone's operation ID/timestamp, and emits an authoritative-operation override. Download validation resolves that override against the current durable winner, and the Windows client rewrites the transported snapshot operation to `D` before the existing atomic exact-primary-key delete. This repairs retained historical zombies without inferring deletes or allowing stale snapshots to resurrect data. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_full_union_zombie_row_reasserts_existing_durable_tombstone`<br>`sync_windows_agent/test/live_sync_api_test.dart::multi-writer download reasserts durable tombstone over zombie full row`<br>`tests/docker-sync/run_scenarios.py::durable-delete-reasserts-against-zombie-full-row`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_durable_tombstone_reasserts_against_zombie_snapshot_row` |

| INC-145 | The Standard verification gate intermittently stopped in the Windows updater rollback test while reading `update.log`, reporting that another process was using the file. | The asynchronous updater briefly held the log without file-sharing access while the polling test performed an unguarded `Get-Content`; the expected transient writer/reader overlap was treated as a product failure. | The rollback test must tolerate a bounded transient Windows sharing violation while continuing to fail on persistent read errors, timeout, missing rollback evidence, or incorrect restored files. | Added a narrowly scoped bounded retry for `System.IO.IOException` when reading the updater log. All semantic rollback assertions and the outer 30-second deadline remain unchanged. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_updater_rollback_log_read_retries_transient_file_sharing`<br>`tests/test_windows_updater_rollback.ps1::Read-UpdateLogWithRetry` |

| INC-146 | The 551-table Sync All completed with four failed downloads: `ce000` and `cp000` on both clients reported `Bad state: Complete snapshots cannot contain explicit delete tombstones`, leaving the known one-row and six-row zombie divergences unchanged. | INC-144 made the server correctly reassert an existing durable explicit tombstone when a canonical full merge positively reported its stale row, but the client still rejected every `D` inside a complete merge. The transport carried the authoritative operation without provenance that distinguished this verified reassertion from an unsafe delete embedded in raw snapshot data. | Complete snapshots must remain non-destructive by absence. They may apply an exact-primary-key delete only when the current server durable winner validates that the operation is an existing explicit `D`; arbitrary or unmarked snapshot deletes must fail atomically, and the row-count guard may permit no more removals than those validated tombstones. | The server now emits a durable-tombstone-reassertion proof only after resolving the candidate against the current durable `D` winner. The client derives a transport-only trust marker from that accepted-operation envelope, rejects unmarked complete-snapshot deletes, and atomically sends only trusted exact-key deletes through the existing tombstone path. The complete-merge row-count invariant remains bounded by the number of validated tombstones, so absence cannot remove rows. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_full_union_zombie_row_reasserts_existing_durable_tombstone`<br>`tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_complete_reconciliation_preserves_target_only_rows_and_verifies_incoming`<br>`sync_windows_agent/test/live_sync_api_test.dart::multi-writer download reasserts durable tombstone over zombie full row`<br>`sync_windows_agent/test/sql_sync_merge_test.dart::only marked explicit deletes are durable tombstone reassertions`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_complete_union_accepts_only_server_verified_tombstone_reassertions` |

| INC-147 | The exact-commit Standard gate intermittently failed its Windows updater rollback test with `Failed startup did not trigger rollback`, even though isolated repeats could pass and the updater had begun startup verification normally. | The test observed `update.log` for only 30 seconds, but the production supervisor intentionally retries an immediately failing executable up to 45 one-second attempts before returning failure to the finalizer. Depending on whether the invalid process survived the first 500 ms probe, rollback either happened quickly or correctly began only after the test had already timed out. The assertion hid the partial log, making the valid in-progress state look like missing rollback. | The rollback observer must outlive every bounded production startup retry plus rollback verification, and timeout failures must include the final updater log so a slow bounded path is distinguishable from a missing rollback. | Extended the isolated observer deadline to 75 seconds, beyond the supervisor's 45-attempt bound and the restored-client stability check, while keeping the production retry behavior unchanged. Failure assertions now include the final collected log. A contract pins the relationship between the supervisor bound and observer deadline. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_updater_rollback_observer_outlives_supervisor_retry_bound`<br>`tests/test_windows_updater_rollback.ps1::Final update log: $logText` |

| INC-148 | After the corrective full unions for `ce000` and `cp000` uploaded successfully, both clients rejected the downloads. The tables stayed divergent, but automatic sync never retried them even though both clients were online, automatic sync was enabled, and the configured intervals had elapsed. | The loop-prevention guard suppressed every fingerprint-divergent table whenever its latest completed upload was a full union. It did not distinguish a successfully applied union from a batch whose download phase failed, while the failed batch had already cleared pending Change Tracking state. The table therefore had no remaining automatic admission path. | A failed download from the latest full-union batch must receive one durable automatic retry after the normal interval. A persistent failure must not create an endless transfer loop, and a successfully completed union that leaves a protected target-only difference must remain suppressed. | Sync jobs now carry internal automatic-retry provenance. The scheduler correlates the latest completed union upload with failed downloads from the same batch, schedules one tagged retry, and durably marks the original failure as scheduled. A retry batch is never automatically retried again; later manual or genuinely new delta activity remains eligible under the existing rules. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_failed_union_download_gets_one_bounded_automatic_retry`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_failed_full_download_receives_only_one_automatic_retry` |

| INC-149 | The first immutable production image build stopped before creating any image because GitHub closed the SSH connection while the builder refreshed `origin/master` for the backend submodule. | The remote-reachability prerequisite treated one transient `git fetch` transport failure as terminal even though image push and remote Kubernetes operations already use bounded retries. | A transient remote refresh failure must receive a small bounded retry before image construction; a persistently unavailable remote or a commit that is not on remote master must still stop the build before any image or deployment mutation. | The existing checked exponential-backoff helper now wraps each root/submodule `git fetch` with three attempts. The ancestry proof remains a separate mandatory check after a successful refresh, so retries cannot admit an unpushed commit or recommend force-pushing. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_production_builder_retries_transient_remote_refresh` |

| INC-150 | The immutable backend build rejected the new automatic retry implementation with 24 compile diagnostics before any image publication. The two primary errors were `db.updateMany is not allowed inside loops/lambdas in strict mode` and `ternary expects matching branch types`; the remaining diagnostics were cascading exclusions. | Source-text contracts cannot execute the TRU strict compiler. The scheduler performed its durable marker update directly inside its table loop, and optional retry provenance used a `null`/string ternary whose branches have different static types. | Scheduler database mutations must respect strict-mode loop rules, and optional string fields must use a single static value type. The immutable `TRU_VALIDATE_ONLY=1` build remains the authoritative compiler gate and must stop publication on any diagnostic. | Moved the exact failed-job marker update into a dedicated non-loop helper called by the scheduler, and store the empty string for ordinary job provenance instead of a mixed null/string ternary. Source regressions pin both strict-mode-safe shapes; the failed build published no image and changed no workload. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_failed_download_retry_uses_tru_strict_mode_safe_shapes`<br>`tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_failed_union_download_gets_one_bounded_automatic_retry` |

| INC-151 | The automatic `ce000` retry reached the corrected client and applied its server-validated tombstone, but both download jobs then failed with `1 incoming row(s) were not applied and were not protected local changes`. | Post-apply complete-snapshot verification compared every transported operation against rows currently present in SQL Server. A validated explicit `D` is correctly absent after application, so the verifier misclassified that expected absence as an unapplied snapshot row. The earlier trust and row-count guards had already limited deletion to the exact server-validated tombstone. | Content equality verification must cover every upsert snapshot row but must not expect a validated delete tombstone to remain as a SQL row after apply. Untrusted deletes must continue failing before merge, snapshot absence must never imply deletion, and exact-key delete application must remain atomic. | Added a single verification-row selector that excludes only operations carrying the server-validated durable-tombstone proof; normal upserts and untrusted deletes remain subject to content checks. The complete snapshot verifier now uses that selector, and the Windows client release is advanced to `1.0.286+290`. | `sync_windows_agent/test/sql_sync_merge_test.dart::complete snapshot content verification excludes only validated deletes`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_validated_full_snapshot_delete_is_not_content_verified_as_a_row` |

| INC-152 | A small divergence in a large table made Velvet Factory download thousands of protocol rows (for example 3,816 entries) because the anti-entropy planner could identify only that the complete table fingerprints differed, not where they differed. | Complete-table fingerprints were monolithic. Once Change Tracking had no pending delta but inventories still differed, the only safe repair was an all-client full union, so unchanged rows consumed bandwidth and apply time. | When every participating client reports a fresh compatible manifest for the same complete inventory, anti-entropy must compare deterministic primary-key ranges and union only differing ranges. Missing, stale, incompatible, or fully divergent manifests must fall back to the proven full union. Snapshot absence must never delete data, and only a server-validated durable explicit tombstone may delete an exact primary key. | Windows clients now publish a versioned 16-bucket primary-key manifest alongside the complete checksum. The shared manual/automatic planner emits a selective range-union job only when every manifest matches its reported row count and checksum and fewer than all ranges differ. Clients still take one consistent full read to prove the manifest, but upload only selected ranges, then recompute the physical target fingerprint after atomic apply. The existing winner register, tombstone proof, target-only-row preservation, and full-union fallback remain authoritative. Client release advances to `1.0.287+291`. | `sync_windows_agent/test/sql_sync_fingerprint_test.dart::range manifest localizes one changed row to one primary-key bucket`<br>`sync_windows_agent/test/sql_sync_fingerprint_test.dart::selective range source is parsed strictly and filters deterministically`<br>`sync_windows_agent/test/sync_state_protocol_test.dart::range fingerprint manifest survives state serialization`<br>`tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_range_manifest_selects_only_differing_primary_key_buckets_with_safe_fallback`<br>`tests/docker-sync/run_scenarios.py::selective-range-three-client-convergence`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_selective_range_union_is_safe_and_backward_compatible` |

| INC-153 | The first exact backend image build for selective range reconciliation stopped at the authoritative TRU validation layer before image publication. | `sync_range_manifest_parts` declared a nullable `map<json>?` argument without the explicit `= null` default required by TRU for every optional parameter. Dart/Python source tests cannot enforce this parser rule. | Every nullable TRU parameter must declare its explicit default, and the immutable `TRU_VALIDATE_ONLY=1` image layer must continue to stop a release before registry or Kubernetes mutation on any parser/type diagnostic. | Added the explicit `= null` default to the range-manifest helper and pinned that strict-compatible declaration in the incident regression. The failed local-only build produced and pushed no image and changed no workload. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_range_manifest_helper_declares_tru_optional_default`<br>`backend/Dockerfile::TRU_VALIDATE_ONLY=1` |
| INC-154 | The first live selective reconciliation for `en000` correctly localized divergence to 14 of 16 ranges, but each client still downloaded about 18.9 MB/18,662 raw inventory rows even though the durable winner filter accepted only 31 uploaded rows per client and the final SQL merge applied 0 rows. | Multi-writer upload records already reduced every stored chunk to its accepted durable-operation metadata, but download pagination selected every raw storage payload by batch ID. Chunks with zero accepted winners therefore crossed the WAN, were decompressed, and were discarded by the client-side accepted-operation filter. Progress also counted raw participant inventory rows instead of relayable winner rows. | A canonical union may relay only chunks containing at least one accepted durable winner, and a chunk whose candidates all became stale must omit its payload. Transfer totals must count relayable rows. An empty optimized full merge must remain a complete insert/update-only verification: snapshot absence must not delete rows, and server-validated explicit tombstones must remain deliverable. | Canonical download pagination now filters durable relay records to `rowCount > 0`, totals only those accepted rows, and omits a selected payload if final winner validation leaves no accepted operation. Full-merge semantics are forced even when every payload is pruned, preserving target-only rows and explicit-delete-only behavior while eliminating the observed no-effect bulk download. | `tests/test_control_plane_contracts.py::test_canonical_union_relays_only_chunks_with_accepted_winner_rows`<br>`sync_windows_agent/test/live_sync_api_test.dart::multi-writer download preserves an empty pruned canonical full merge`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_zero_winner_union_chunks_are_not_downloaded`<br>`tests/docker-sync/run_scenarios.py::accepted-winner-chunk-pruning-three-client-safety` |
| INC-155 | Recent large-table jobs showed that safe table uploads were processed sequentially with one fixed package/compression policy even when the link was healthy; this left independent-table WAN and SQL-read latency hidden and made recovery behavior identical for a fast connection and a recently failing connection. | The protocol already had immutable chunk IDs, durable retries, verified disk caching, atomic SQL staging, range fingerprints, and delta/full fallback, but it had no conservative transport feedback policy and did not overlap independent upload jobs. | Healthy links may process only a small bounded number of independent table uploads, while any request failure or sustained latency must immediately fall back to one smaller strongly-compressed package at a time. Chunks inside one table batch and all target downloads must remain ordered. Retries must keep the same durable identity, and target SQL must remain untouched until the existing final atomic merge. | Added a hysteretic adaptive transfer policy: healthy transport runs at most two independent table uploads with fast gzip; failure/latency throttles later waves to one smaller package with stronger gzip until four healthy acknowledgements. Per-table packages stay sequential and only their last package carries the final marker. Downloads remain dependency-ordered and serial, preserving the existing disk checkpoints, checksum verification, selective range planning, commit ledger, Change Tracking fallback, atomic SQL apply, and post-commit cursor advancement. Client release advances to `1.0.288+292`. | `sync_windows_agent/test/sync_transfer_policy_test.dart::bounded runner preserves result order and concurrency ceiling`<br>`sync_windows_agent/test/sync_transfer_policy_test.dart::one failure throttles immediately and recovery is hysteretic`<br>`sync_windows_agent/test/delta_package_test.dart::adaptive gzip levels preserve the exact payload`<br>`sync_windows_agent/test/live_sync_api_test.dart::downloadSnapshot surfaces retry exhaustion for manifest failures`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_adaptive_parallel_transfer_preserves_atomic_finalization`<br>`tests/docker-sync/run.ps1` |
| INC-156 | The first focused test compile for the new generic bounded-transfer runner stopped before executing because Dart rejected `const <T>[]`: a runtime type parameter cannot appear in a const literal. | The empty-work fast path used an otherwise harmless const-list optimization without accounting for Dart's restriction on generic const type arguments. | Generic transfer helpers must compile for every result type, return an empty typed result without invoking work, and remain exercised by the focused and standard client suites before publication. | Replaced the invalid generic const literal with a normal typed empty list. The focused policy/package suite now compiles and passes, and a source regression prevents the invalid shape from returning. No artifact or live workload was changed by the failed local test. | `sync_windows_agent/test/sync_transfer_policy_test.dart::invalid parallelism fails before running work`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_generic_bounded_runner_empty_result_is_not_const` |
| INC-157 | Pre-release review of the first parallel-upload draft found that it overlapped packages inside one multi-writer batch. | INC-396 | The first focused delta-export regression run failed because the collector test still searched for the retired `$backupPath` destination variable. | The collector now supports both backup and delta artifacts through `$artifactPath`, but the ordering assertion remained coupled to the old backup-only name. | The regression must continue proving that a complete manifest is present before any output artifact is created while following the current format-neutral variable. | Updated the assertion to locate `$artifactPath` and reran the exact focused suite successfully. | `tests/test_sync_contracts.py::test_live_copy_collector_restarts_after_frontend_pod_replacement` |
| INC-397 | The first combined delta regression suite found two more stale documentation contracts: one still expected `backup length mismatch`, and one still claimed no database mutation after the user had deliberately created one controlled sale. | The implementation and progress state advanced to format-neutral exports and a user-entered laboratory transaction, but the historical assertions remained coupled to the earlier backup-only/pre-input wording. | Incident coverage must preserve checksum-before-cleanup and no-automated-input safety without denying the explicitly recorded manual sale. | Updated the collector ordering assertion to the format-neutral length check and narrowed the safety assertion to no automated Al-Ameen input, restore, or synchronization; the complete combined suite was rerun. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_live_copy_chunks_survive_until_whole_backup_is_verified`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_alameen_lab_progress_records_command_delivery_blocker` |

| INC-426 | The first read-only recovery inspection pod failed with permission denied on `global/pg_control`. | The inspection manifest assumed UID/GID 999, but the Alpine PostgreSQL volume is owned by numeric UID 70 and mixed GIDs; the root-owned backup parent also initially lacked traverse permission. | Read-only recovery tooling must derive the data-directory UID/GID from the preserved source and must never guess an image-user identity. | Preserved ownership with `cp -a`, granted only traverse permission on the recovery parent, derived numeric ownership from `stat`, and added a reusable inspection helper that mounts the backup read-only under that identity. | `scripts/inspect_production_postgres_backup.ps1`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_postgres_wal_recovery_preserves_physical_and_logical_backups` |
| INC-427 | The initial short-lived inspection command waited for Pod Ready and timed out even though an inspection pod should terminate rather than remain Ready. | A service-pod readiness condition was incorrectly used for a finite diagnostic pod. | Finite recovery pods must be polled to terminal phase and must require `Succeeded`, not Ready. | Replaced the Ready wait with bounded phase polling over Kubernetes JSON and explicit `Succeeded` enforcement. | `scripts/inspect_production_postgres_backup.ps1`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_postgres_wal_recovery_preserves_physical_and_logical_backups` |
| INC-428 | The first isolated-candidate verification launcher found an unterminated single-quoted PowerShell here-string; no verification or database write ran. | The closing marker was written as a lone quote instead of the required `'@`, and the ad-hoc launcher did not stop before spawning its child. | Recovery helpers must pass PowerShell parser validation before launch, and here-string delimiters must be syntactically complete. | Corrected the terminator, used strict parser validation before relaunch, and retained the helper in the incident regression suite. | `scripts/verify_production_postgres_recovery_candidate.ps1`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_postgres_wal_recovery_preserves_physical_and_logical_backups` |
| INC-429 | The first corrected candidate verifier exited without producing a dump because its encoded script was passed through a fragile SSH `sh -c` argument boundary. | The command still depended on PowerShell/SSH/remote-shell argument reconstruction, so the intended script was not delivered as one stdin program. | Multi-line recovery commands must be streamed through stdin to `kubectl exec -i -- sh`, with native exit status enforced. | Replaced the encoded `sh -c` invocation with direct stdin streaming; the rerun completed `pg_amcheck`, `pg_dump`, `pg_restore --list`, and the bounded database summary. | `scripts/verify_production_postgres_recovery_candidate.ps1`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_postgres_wal_recovery_preserves_physical_and_logical_backups` |
| INC-430 | A post-repair ad-hoc `psql -Atc` query failed in remote Bash on SQL parentheses even though PostgreSQL itself had rolled out Ready. | The SQL was sent through the same unsafe PowerShell/SSH/Bash quoting boundary seen in INC-410 and INC-421. | Production SQL verification must stream SQL over stdin and treat a quoting failure as no database evidence. | Repeated the database-read check through `kubectl exec -i` stdin; it succeeded before the backend was restored. | `scripts/verify_production_postgres_recovery_candidate.ps1`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_postgres_wal_recovery_preserves_physical_and_logical_backups` |
| INC-431 | The first public health request immediately after backend rollout returned nginx 503, and the ad-hoc PowerShell sequence continued to print a false-valued object with process exit 0. | Kubernetes rollout readiness completed before the ingress endpoint transition settled, while the shell did not set terminating error behavior for `Invoke-RestMethod`. | Release health verification must use a bounded retry, require Ready, database availability, zero compile errors, and the exact immutable commit, and end nonzero if the invariant is not reached. | Verified production through a strict bounded retry, then added the same 90-second invariant gate to the reusable credential rotation helper. | `scripts/rotate_production_postgres_credentials.ps1::Wait-ProductionHealth`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_postgres_credential_rotation_is_coordinated_and_rollback_capable` |
| INC-432 | The first combined recovery/chart verification reported `No module named pytest`, but later commands still ran and the shell returned success. | The current Python environment lacks optional pytest, and PowerShell does not automatically convert a native nonzero exit code into a terminating error even with `$ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'`. | Repository unittest files must be directly runnable without pytest, and compound native verification must check `$LASTEXITCODE` after each command rather than relying on PowerShell exception settings. | Treated the pytest attempt as failed evidence, ran the chart tests through their dependency-free unittest entry point, and kept incident completeness as the regression for the observed launcher failure. | `deployment/chart/tests/test_chart_contracts.py`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_every_catalog_incident_has_existing_automated_coverage` |
| INC-433 | The first live scheduled logical-backup Job produced and listed its dump but failed checksum verification, then Kubernetes started a retry of the same Job. | Alpine's BusyBox `sha256sum` supports the portable short `-c` flag but not GNU coreutils' long `--check` option. | The PostgreSQL backup image's exact checksum syntax must be covered, and a failed Job must never be accepted as a completed backup. | Stopped the waiting verifier, deleted only the failed verification Job, replaced `--check` with BusyBox-compatible `-c`, and extended the chart contract to reject the unsupported flag before redeployment. | `deployment/chart/templates/postgres-backup-cronjob.yaml`<br>`deployment/chart/tests/test_chart_contracts.py::test_postgres_has_validated_retained_logical_backups_on_separate_pvc` |
| INC-434 | The first corrected-backup redeploy command was rejected locally before execution because it combined deployment with deletion of prior log files. | The command violated the execution safety policy by including destructive log cleanup in an otherwise valid launch sequence. | Verification retries must preserve prior evidence logs, use new versioned filenames, and separate deployment from process launch. | Confirmed no production action occurred, preserved the failed-attempt logs, applied the corrected CronJob separately, and launched the retry with `-v2` log files. | `scripts/verify_production_postgres_backup_job.ps1`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_every_catalog_incident_has_existing_automated_coverage` |
| INC-435 | A follow-up query intended to inspect only one service-account Secret's metadata again printed its encoded data values in local parser errors. | Full multiline Secret JSON was piped into `ForEach-Object`, so `ConvertFrom-Json` received individual lines and included those lines—including `.data` values—in its error messages. | Secret diagnostics must never retrieve full Secret JSON when server-side metadata projection can answer the question; metadata tooling must include type and service-account owner without rendering `.data` values. Any exposed token must be immediately revoked. | Stopped the unsafe pattern, extended the server-side go-template metadata helper with Secret type and service-account owner, confirmed the token had no workload consumer, and revoked the compromised service-account token. | `scripts/list_production_secret_metadata.ps1`<br>`scripts/revoke_unused_production_service_account_token.ps1`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_production_secret_metadata_helper_never_prints_secret_values` |
| INC-436 | After the service-account token Secret was successfully deleted, its first absence check called `.Trim()` on the expected null output and emitted an error before printing a true result. | `kubectl get --ignore-not-found` returns no pipeline object for an absent resource, not an empty string object. | Absence verification must join possibly empty native output into a string before testing it and must not report success from a sequence that emitted an error. | Repeated the check with null-safe joining, confirmed the token Secret is absent, and added a reusable revocation helper that uses the same guarded absence logic. | `scripts/revoke_unused_production_service_account_token.ps1`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_production_secret_metadata_helper_never_prints_secret_values` |
| INC-437 | The first Cloud Bootstrap registry workflow was rejected locally before process creation because a very large inline command contained recursive temporary-directory cleanup. | Packaging credential handling, Kubernetes mutation, verification, and cleanup into one shell command made the safety boundary opaque to the execution policy. Bootstrap was not fetched and production was unchanged. | Cloud Bootstrap must be fetched exactly once by a repository-owned, parse-checked helper; credentials and kubeconfig must remain in memory or a validated temporary directory; cleanup must be scoped inside that helper; and registry login, `/v2/` HTTP 200, pull-Secret update, and an Always-pull immutable image probe are all required. | Moved the complete workflow into a guarded script that reads the ignored `.cloud.env`, suppresses credential-bearing errors, retries login with fresh Docker configs, applies only the namespace pull Secret, verifies an immutable pull, and removes its validated temporary directory. | `scripts/rotate_production_registry_from_cloud_bootstrap.ps1`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_cloud_bootstrap_registry_rotation_is_secret_safe_and_pull_verified` |
| INC-438 | The first guarded Cloud registry helper stopped before fetching Bootstrap because its parameter default resolved `.cloud.env` as `\..\.cloud.env`. | `$PSScriptRoot` is not available when that parameter default expression is evaluated in Windows PowerShell 5.1. | Repository-relative secret paths must be resolved in the script body after automatic variables are initialized; a pre-fetch path failure must not consume the one permitted Bootstrap request. | Changed the parameter default to empty, resolved and normalized the ignored `.cloud.env` from `$PSScriptRoot` in the body, and confirmed the failure occurred before any HTTP or production operation. | `scripts/rotate_production_registry_from_cloud_bootstrap.ps1`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_cloud_bootstrap_registry_rotation_is_secret_safe_and_pull_verified` |
| INC-439 | The one permitted Cloud Bootstrap request succeeded, but the registry helper stopped before login because strict-mode access to missing `kubernetes.namespace` threw before its `namespaceName` fallback ran. | Optional/fallback property access was written as direct member access under `Set-StrictMode -Version Latest`; the successful Bootstrap response stayed only in the ended process and was not logged or persisted. | Bootstrap schema fallbacks must inspect `PSObject.Properties` before member access. A successfully consumed one-fetch contract must never be silently fetched again unless Cloud explicitly documents the retry as idempotent or supplies a new token. | Added property-existence guards for canonical `namespaceName` and its temporary alias. After Cloud explicitly made same-token GET retry idempotent and the user supplied a new token, the guarded retry validated schema v1, registry login, authenticated `/v2/`, pull-Secret application, and an Always-pull immutable image. | `scripts/rotate_production_registry_from_cloud_bootstrap.ps1`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_cloud_bootstrap_registry_rotation_is_secret_safe_and_pull_verified` |
| INC-440 | With the corrected Cloud v1 schema, Bootstrap, registry login, and authenticated `/v2/` succeeded, but comparison with the existing `regcred` stopped on a missing optional `username` member before Secret mutation. | The existing Docker config uses the valid compact `auth`-only representation. Strict mode rejected direct access to absent expanded `username`/`password` fields before the decoder fallback ran. | Pull-secret comparison must support both expanded Docker credential fields and the standard base64 `auth` field through property-existence checks. | Confirmed the Cloud response exposes `cloud.deployment.bootstrap.v1`, canonical `namespaceName`, its compatibility alias, and every documented registry field; guarded all optional legacy Docker config members before decoding `auth`; retained the idempotent retry path. | `scripts/rotate_production_registry_from_cloud_bootstrap.ps1`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_cloud_bootstrap_registry_rotation_is_secret_safe_and_pull_verified` |
| INC-441 | The first multi-file patch for the legacy Docker-auth fix was rejected before changing any file because an empty hunk separator made the following file header invalid patch content. | The patch contained a stray `@@` boundary immediately before the next `Update File` section. | A rejected security patch is not an applied fix; multi-file corrections must use independently valid hunks and be verified before retrying production access. | Confirmed no partial edit occurred, split the source and regression/documentation changes into valid patches, and reran parser and incident checks before the idempotent Bootstrap retry. | `tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_cloud_bootstrap_registry_rotation_is_secret_safe_and_pull_verified`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_every_catalog_incident_has_existing_automated_coverage` |
| INC-442 | The first background deployment progress poll serialized a single `Get-Content` result with PowerShell provider-extended properties, producing a very large diagnostic object instead of a bounded log tail. | Raw pipeline objects were passed to `ConvertTo-Json`; a one-line result retained provider metadata that JSON serialization traversed recursively. | Background-task monitoring must convert log tails to plain joined strings before JSON serialization and must never expose provider metadata or expand the requested output scope. | Added a reusable bounded log-tail reader that joins and casts both streams to plain strings before emitting compact JSON, and used the same safe conversion for subsequent deployment monitoring. | `scripts/read_background_task_log_tail.ps1`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_background_log_tail_is_plain_text_before_json_serialization` |
| INC-443 | The first Cloud Bootstrap Helm upgrade stopped during rendering with `nil pointer evaluating interface {}.enabled` for `postgresBackup`, after both immutable images had already been pushed. Production remained on the prior healthy release. | `helm upgrade --reuse-values` discarded newly introduced chart defaults when the installed legacy release had no `postgresBackup` value tree, so the backup template dereferenced an absent map. | Production upgrades must start with current chart defaults, overlay retained live values, preserve explicit immutable-image overrides, and roll back atomically if a later upgrade stage fails. | Replaced `--reuse-values` with Helm's `--reset-then-reuse-values`, added `--rollback-on-failure`, and extended the Cloud deployment contract regression to reject the unsafe legacy flag. | `scripts/deploy_production_via_cloud_bootstrap.ps1`<br>`tests/test_incident_regressions.py::test_cloud_bootstrap_registry_rotation_is_secret_safe_and_pull_verified` |
| INC-444 | An independent post-deployment query reported NotFound for `cronjob/sql-sync-auto-scheduler` while all requested production health checks otherwise passed. | The diagnostic guessed the scheduler resource name from its Secret instead of using the chart's rendered component name, `sql-sync-auto-tick`. | Production verification must use chart-owned resource identities and must distinguish a diagnostic naming error from a missing scheduler. | Listed namespace CronJobs, confirmed `sql-sync-auto-tick` is active and had succeeded in the current minute, and extended the chart contract to pin the rendered scheduler component name. | `deployment/chart/templates/auto-scheduler-cronjob.yaml`<br>`deployment/chart/tests/test_chart_contracts.py::test_auto_scheduler_is_not_deployed_suspended` |

## Coverage limits

### INC-349 follow-up evidence (2026-09-03)

A controlled read-only delta on `alshallan2` resolved one concrete source of `ma000` equal-count fingerprint divergence. Al-Ameen emitted 12 explicit deletes at Change Tracking version `5763` and 12 inserts at `5764`. Comparison with the verified restored baseline proved that every non-primary business field was identical while all physical GUID identities changed; the row count remained 12 and SQL Server declares no foreign key referencing `ma000`. This is identity churn, not evidence that rows went missing. The existing long-term safety rule remains correct: preserve explicit operations and causal ordering, never infer deletes from a snapshot, and never coalesce or pick an authority for `ma000` until a table-specific business identity is proven. Evidence and the next experimental baseline are recorded in `docs/alameen-sales-observation-2026-09-03.md`.

Local Docker tests validate the synchronization architecture and SQL transaction semantics. They cannot perfectly simulate Windows UAC, every SQL Server installation permission model, native GPU/window behavior, physical power removal, or arbitrary WAN failure. Those boundaries require isolated Windows virtual machines or explicitly isolated fake live clients; they never justify testing against active production databases.
